# Welcome

Welcome to Analog Docs—your comprehensive resource for everything Analog, whether you’re a user, developer, or node operator.

Analog is a sovereign, interoperability-first network that aims to unite autonomous chains by providing interoperability, liquidity, and automated trading. Read the [Timepaper](https://www.analog.one/Analog-Timepaper.pdf) for more details.

# Overview

## What Is Analog?

Analog is a sovereign blockchain designed to power the next era of decentralized ecosystems. Built as an interoperability-first network, Analog unites fragmented chains by solving ***cross-chain communication and liquidity*** challenges. Analog aims to bridge traditional finance (TradFi) and Web3, enabling developers to build ***future-proof dApps*** that transcend chain boundaries.

Check out [Protocol Overview](/documentation/developers/protocol-overview) for more details.

***

## Why Analog?

The increasing demand for blockspace has resulted in a multi-chain ecosystem where each blockchain is effectively a “*walled garden*” with little to no connectivity with other blockchain networks.

While Ethereum and other smart contract blockchains have attempted to address scalability issues by distributing users, data, and funds across multiple parallel domains (e.g., rollups, sidechains, and app chains), this approach has introduced new problems: a *fragmented UX*, *fragmented data*, and *fragmented liquidity*, ultimately jeopardizing the core principles of decentralization.

Analog's architecture provides **seamless cross-chain interoperability** through these core components:

* **Timechain**: Built on Substrate SDK, Timechain serves as a central coordination layer ("Accountability Layer") that manages all cross-chain activities. It ensures secure governance, validator efficiency, and transaction finality. See [Protocol Overview](/documentation/developers/protocol-overview) for more details.&#x20;
* **Timechain EVM:** Extends Analog's capabilities by allowing developers to run native Solidity smart contracts directly on the Timechain.  It uses PolkaVM and pallet-revive to efficiently run Solidity smart contracts natively on Substrate. See [Timechain EVM ](/documentation/developers/timechain-evm/technology-and-architecture)for more details.&#x20;
* **Analog GMP**: Securely transfers [messages](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/getting-started) and [tokens](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/tutorials/teleport-tokens-from-chain-a-to-chain-b) between different blockchains. Developers/token creators can also leverage the [Omni-Chain Analog Token Standard(OATS)](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/building-with-oats) to simplify the management of tokens across multiple blockchains.&#x20;

***

## Why Build on Analog?

Analog empowers developers to build multi-chain/cross-chain apps that seamlessly interact with any supported blockchain. From Ethereum and Astar to BNB and Polygon (with future support for Solana and TON), Analog provides the infrastructure to make this possible with minimal friction, with features such as:

<details>

<summary>Seamless Developer Experience</summary>

Analog provides a powerful yet simple DevEx, offering a comprehensive suite of tools and protocols to help you build the next generation of dApps:

* **Analog GMP**: A core protocol for all cross-chain communication within the Analog ecosystem. See [Analog GMP](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp) for more details.&#x20;
* **OATS**: A core standard built on top of Analog GMP that allows you to build tokens that operate seamlessly across multiple chains. See [OATS](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/building-with-oats) for more details.&#x20;
* **Timechain EVM**: Seamlessly deploy and execute your Solidity smart contracts directly on the Timechain. See [Timechain EVM](/documentation/developers/timechain-evm/technology-and-architecture) for more details.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Chain Abstraction</strong></summary>

A key feature of Analog’s products, such as [Analog GMP](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp) and [Timechain EVM](/documentation/developers/timechain-evm/technology-and-architecture), is their ability to simplify and manage complex logic across any supported chain. This eliminates the need for users to switch chains, sign transactions on multiple networks, or spend gas on each.&#x20;

By leveraging this capability, you can completely abstract the underlying chain from your users, freeing them from the need to be aware of which chain they are interacting with.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Future-Proofing</strong></summary>

Developing your app with Analog’s products, such as [Analog GMP](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp) or [Timechain EVM](/documentation/developers/timechain-evm/technology-and-architecture), future-proofs it for new blockchain integrations. Your app automatically gains access to users and assets on newly supported blockchains, eliminating the need for contract updates or redeployment.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Fully Composable</strong></summary>

Analog's products, such as [GMP](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp), are fully composable protocols designed to enable seamless cross-chain interactions while ensuring transaction integrity across multiple networks. For example, developers can use [Analog GMP](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp) in conjunction with [Circle CCTP](https://developers.circle.com/stablecoins/cctp-getting-started) to build applications that facilitate atomic payload transfers alongside native cross-chain transactions.&#x20;

For more details, see [Zenswap](https://docs.zenswap.io/).

</details>

***

## Use Cases: What You Can Build

* **Cross-Chain DeFi:** Launch **omnichain memecoins**, enable low-slippage swaps via [**Zenswap**](https://zenswap.io/), or protocols that amplify yields.&#x20;
* **RWA Tokenization:** Tokenize real estate, invoices, or supply chains with RWA Synthetic Assets, backed by deep liquidity.
* **NFTs & Creators:** Let users mint, fractionalize, and transfer NFTs across chains.&#x20;
* **Sustainable Yield:** Offer users stable returns tied to real revenue streams via the Analog Yield Vault.

***


# Getting Started


# Wallets & Accounts

## Setting Up a Substrate Wallet

Analog currently offers support for wallets based on the Substrate framework. See [Supported Wallets ](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets)for more details. For step-by-step guidance on setting up Substrate accounts using the various tools developed and supported by Web3 Foundation, refer to the [official documentation](https://support.polkadot.network/support/solutions/articles/65000098878-how-to-create-a-dot-account).

{% hint style="info" %}
As a Substrate-based network, Analog adopts the [SS58 address format](https://docs.substrate.io/reference/address-formats/), a Substrate standard for encoding account identifiers. This base-58 encoding scheme enables interoperability between Analog and other Substrate-based blockchains, including Kusama and its parachains. Analog addresses are uniquely identifiable by the “an” prefix.

For more details about generating ss58 addresses with different Supported Wallets, see [Add Support for Analog to Existing Substrate Wallets](#add-support-for-analog-to-the-existing-substrate-wallets).&#x20;
{% endhint %}

## Add Support for Analog to the Existing Substrate Wallets

This tutorial will guide you through the process of enabling Analog support for your existing [Substrate wallets](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets).&#x20;

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Talisman" %}

1. Launch your Talisman extension.
2. Add Analog/Timechain support to your Talisman extension by navigating to “*Settings*” -> ”*Network & Tokens*” -> “*Manage Networks*” and adding the following network details:
   * Network Name: Analog Timechain
   * RPC URL: [wss://rpc.timechain.analog.one](wss://rpc.mainnet.analog.one/)
   * Explorer: <https://explorer.analog.one/>
3. To locate your Timechain address ([SS58 address](#address-format)), click on “Copy Address” and search for “Analog.”&#x20;

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2Fo7PN7Od4WfNgfQi80dLx%2FScreenshot%202025-02-12%20at%2011.35.21.png?alt=media&amp;token=ea27ada8-856a-4f30-b0e4-c64b2526b166" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Fearless" %}

1. Open your Fearless extension.
2. Navigate to “All networks” and search for “Analog.”
3. Navigate to the “Analog Timechain” option and toggle the button next to it.

   Once toggled, “Analog Timechain” will be added to your favorites list.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FptKoh5jAhLVD8dYdgdNn%2F3%20by%202.png?alt=media&amp;token=ddb40cb3-841c-4fcb-ba71-3226eb757e7b" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

To locate your Timechain address (SS58 address), click the “Wallet” button at the bottom and type “ANLOG” into the search box. You should be able to see your address as shown here:&#x20;

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2F7Z3M1A5m9sqrjr3wPv4x%2FScreenshot%202025-01-27%20at%2002.28.23.png?alt=media&amp;token=37bc05d5-dc0c-44a6-8264-74efd50be80b" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="SubWallet" %}

1. Launch your Subwallet extension.
2. Add Analog/Timechain support to your SubWallet extension by navigating to “*Settings*” -> ”*Manage networks*”  and adding the following network details:
   * Provider URL: [wss://rpc.timechain.analog.one](wss://rpc.mainnet.analog.one/)
   * Network name: Analog Timechain
   * Symbol: ANLOG
   * Block explorer: <https://explorer.analog.one/>
3. Once Analog Timechain has been added, you should see ANLOG on the wallet assets page.
4. Click on ANLOG and copy the Timechain-compatible address.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2F4wgBvPoakA1EZh15X83v%2FScreenshot%202025-02-12%20at%2012.12.23.png?alt=media&amp;token=cf43c5cb-861e-4fa9-bef9-a94571e31fba" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Polkadot.Js" %}

1. Download the [Polkadot.js Extension](https://polkadot.js.org/extension/) and [create](https://support.polkadot.network/support/solutions/articles/65000180529) an account if you haven’t done so. You can also [import an existing Substrate account into Polkadot.js Extension](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/learn-guides-accounts).
2. Launch the Polkadot.Js Apps and point it to the Analog Timechain by entering this address: <https://console.analog.one/>.&#x20;
3. Allow the Polkadot.js Apps to interact with the Polkadot.js Extension in the prompt window.
4. To find Analog’s SS58 address for your account, navigate to the “[Accounts](https://console.analog.one/#/accounts)” tab, and click on “Copy Address” as shown here:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FdwLbYXvU7iKlQbwGh20A%2FPolkdotjs%20Analog%20Address.png?alt=media&amp;token=f75353a2-b8a7-4240-ab59-7b3219baa001" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

## How To Connect Your Wallet With Analog

To set up a new Analog account on the mainnet (Analog Timechain), ensure you’re on the page at [Polkadot.js Console](https://console.analog.one/) (i.e., <https://console.analog.one/>) and that “**Analog Timechain”** is selected in the top left corner. This is what you should see:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FFAccPZEBsyKZ8N6OzuYq%2FAnalog%20Console.png?alt=media&amp;token=2711d0ee-9bb6-457b-aee0-253804ee69dd" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

That is the default view of Analog’s block explorer, which displays the latest blocks and events. To view all account-related activities, simply switch to the [Accounts](https://console.analog.one/#/accounts) tab. Please note that many [Supported Wallets](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets) features require a funded account in the [Accounts](https://console.analog.one/#/accounts) tab. Without funds, some tabs and menus may be unavailable.&#x20;

{% hint style="success" %}
You can access the same account with multiple wallets (see [Supported Wallets](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets)), provided you’ve provided the same mnemonic seed. To use an existing account (e.g., from SubWallet) in Talisman or Polkadot.js Extension, simply import it—no need to create a new one or transfer assets. See the following links for more details.

* Talisman: <https://docs.talisman.xyz/talisman/start/importing-existing-accounts>
* SubWallet: <https://docs.subwallet.app/main/extension-user-guide/account-management/import-accounts>
* Polkadot.js Extension: <https://support.polkadot.network/support/solutions/articles/65000169952-polkadot-extension-how-to-restore-your-account>
  {% endhint %}

## Existential Deposit

Analog uses an “[existential deposit](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/learn-accounts#existential-deposit-and-reaping)” (ED) mechanism, currently set at `1 ANLOG`, to minimize the storage burden on the Timechain. To conserve on-chain resources, accounts with balances below the ED are deactivated from the Timechain, and their remaining funds are destroyed.

This deactivation does not hinder account access; however, the account’s state is no longer maintained on the Timechain. To reactivate the account and restore its on-chain state, you must deposit an amount exceeding the ED. However, this operation won’t restore the destroyed funds.

Here’s what you need to pay attention to about ED:

* The [Timechain Explorer](/documentation/resources/tooling/timechain-explorer) does not discover accounts that have never received a deposit.
* The transaction will fail if you attempt to transfer funds below the minimum ED to an empty account.
* You can reactivate your account at any time. However, this operation won’t restore the previously destroyed funds.
* When transferring funds, always enable “transfer with account keep-alive checks,” as shown here, to avoid losing funds due to an insufficient ED balance.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FlDKdfOPlrEPEX5o1NybH%2FExistential%20Deposit.png?alt=media&amp;token=3d20a955-56bc-4263-aa61-71b07ae97bf1" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

* If you transfer all (or almost all) of your funds from an account, causing the remaining balance to fall below the minimum ED, the account will be “reaped,” and any remaining funds will be forfeited.
* Most exchanges have minimum deposit amounts, which typically exceed Analog’s ED. Always confirm these amounts with your chosen exchange before transferring funds.

## Cold Storage Wallets

Currently, Analog works only with the [Polkadot Vault](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/polkadot-vault), a mobile app that lets you securely store your crypto. Polkadot Vault has a special “offline mode” where you put your phone on airplane mode to help you keep your funds safe from online threats.&#x20;

Polkadot Vault is available on both iOS and Android platforms. It enables secure transaction signing through QR codes to protect your private keys. It integrates seamlessly with various Substrate-based wallets, including Talisman, SubWallet, Polkadot.JS, and Nova, and provides comprehensive account management features, such as account creation, management, and restoration.

To interact with the Timechain, Polkadot Vault must have the latest runtime metadata. You can find these details in the following links:

* **Analog Timechain** (i.e., Analog Mainnet): <https://metadata.analog.one/#/analog-timechain>
* **Analog Testnet**: <https://metadata.analog.one/#/analog-testnet>

Here are some important links to get you started with Polkadot Vault:

* Creating a new account in Polkadot Vault: <https://support.polkadot.network/support/solutions/articles/65000184076-polkadot-vault-how-to-create-an-account>
* Integrating Polkadot Vault account with Polkadot.JS: <https://support.polkadot.network/support/solutions/articles/65000184118-polkadot-vault-how-to-add-your-account-on-polkadot-js-ui>
* Integrating Polkadot Vault account with SubWallet: <https://docs.subwallet.app/main/mobile-app-user-guide/account-management/attach-a-polkadot-vault-previously-parity-signer-account>
* Integrating Polkadot Vault account with Nova: <https://docs.novawallet.io/nova-wallet-wiki/wallet-management/hardware-wallets/polkadot-vault/add-polkadot-vault-account-to-nova-wallet>


# Multi-sig Accounts

A multi-sig (or multi-signature) account is a special account that requires multiple signatures to sign an extrinsic. Multi-sig accounts significantly enhance security and distribute control of the account. In this section, you will learn how to create and use a multi-sig wallet on Analog Timechain.&#x20;

{% hint style="info" %}
**Note:**

Multi-sig account addresses are *deterministic*, meaning you can always calculate the address of a multi-sig account by knowing the account’s members and threshold. Since the multi-sig is stored on-chain, you cannot modify it after creation. This means changes to members or the threshold are impossible. Instead, you must dissolve the existing multi-sig and create a new one.
{% endhint %}

## How to create a multi-sig account

Suppose Alice, Bob, and Charlie want to create a 2-of-3 multi-sig account to manage their funds jointly. The following steps demonstrate how to proceed.

### Add Contacts

1. Launch [Polkadot.Js Apps](/documentation/resources/tooling/polkadot.js) by visiting <https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/explorer>
2. Navigate to “[***Accounts***”-> ”***Address book.***](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/addresses)”&#x20;
3. Click "`+Add Contact`" to create accounts you want to include in the multi-sig wallet. In this example, we’ll add Alice’s, Bob’s, and Charlie’s addresses and give their contact names each time clicking “***Save***.”
4. You should be able to see the accounts created as shown here:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FUiGhz5faa9Z47aGFFVXj%2FAccounts%20Created.png?alt=media&amp;token=12c460d0-f947-4464-94a5-963cfdc4d979" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

### Create a new multi-sig wallet

1. Navigate to “Accounts”-> “`+Multisig`”

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FP1YS4omVWNFRPIPVTAbG%2FMultisig%20Pallet.png?alt=media&amp;token=f30f63e3-dc45-4249-86db-775f65584ca6" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

2. From the “***Available Signatories***” column, move the accounts you want to include in the multi-sig wallet to the “***Selected Signatories***” column. In this case, we’ll include all the three accounts.
3. Enter the threshold. In this case, we’ll add a threshold of 2, meaning a minimum of 2 signatories are required to sign an extrinsic.
4. Finally, enter the name of the multi-sig account and click on the “***Create***” button.
5. Congratulations! You have just created a multi-sig account. You can see the multi-sig account on the Accounts page as shown here:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FypAPqeLeRbXbmqsuRXxa%2FMulti-sig%20Wallet%20Account.png?alt=media&amp;token=e78fb0ad-4009-4ab8-9e57-c9c63f219086" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

## How to use a multi-sig account

Now that you’ve set up the multi-signature wallet, you can initiate a multi-signature call from one of its members (let's say Alice). In this example, we’ll transfer some ANLOG from the multi-sig account to another member, say Bob.

{% hint style="info" %}
Note that a wallet member (e.g., Alice) must first make a ***`deposit`*** to initiate a multi-signature call. The ***`deposit`*** is locked until the call is executed and serves as a fee for the storage space it takes up on the blockchain. This mechanism discourages users from setting up multi-sig calls that are never executed. The ***`deposit`*** is held within the initiator’s account.

The network determines the deposit value using the formula:

```bash
deposit = depositBase + (threshold * depositFactor)
```

The constants `depositBase`and `depositFactor` are predefined parameters established within the chain’s runtime environment. For more details about Substrate multi-sig accounts, check out this [documentation](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/learn-guides-accounts-multisig).&#x20;
{% endhint %}

Now that the basics are out of the way, let's create a multi-sig call from the multi-sig account. Suppose Alice wants to transfer `0.1 ANLOG` from a multi-sig wallet to Bob’s account. Here's how you'll proceed:

1. Navigate to the “[Accounts”-> “Transfer”](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/extrinsics) page.
2. Select your multi-sig wallet. In this case, we’ll select Demo\_Wallet.
3. Navigate to the “**send to address**” pallet and then choose **Bob**.
4. Enter the amount you wish to transfer. In this case, we’ll enter `0.1 ANLOG`.
5. Next, click the “Make Transfer” link to initiate the transfer process. Here's what you should see:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2F2EiFzAlVFZlddf7QMwL4%2FGet%20the%20Encoded%20Data.png?alt=media&amp;token=c9f53f97-d30b-4c62-b84d-1fb4bbf4ceb9" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

In the screen that appears, you should be able to see:

* The sender account. In this case, the Demo\_Wallet
* The signatory that is signing the extrinsic. In this case, it is Alice.
* The "multi-sig call data," which you’ll need to save for later, as Bob and Charlie will need it to approve the extrinsic.

6. Now copy the "multisig call data" and share it with Bob and Charlie. Next, click “Sign and Submit.”
7. Upon signing the extrinsic, a multisig call is triggered, reserving some deposit (see link for more details in Alice’s account. An icon should appear next to the multisig account, signifying a pending approval for this multi-sig call.
8. Bob or Charlie can now hover over the icon and click “View pending approvals” to approve the transaction. Since this call is a 2-of-3 multi-sig, only one of them is needed to approve the transaction. However, the signatory providing the final approval must paste Alice’s “multisig call data” in step 6 to finalize the call.

{% hint style="info" %}
**Note:**

Before signing the transaction, the final signatory must verify that what they are signing is accurate and that Alice didn’t make a mistake in sharing “multisig call data” with them.

Here are steps to verify the“multisig call data”:

1. Navigate to “[Developer” -> “Extrinsics](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/extrinsics).”
2. Locate the “**Decode**” tab and paste the “multisig call data" in the text box that appears.  This should display exactly what the extrinsic is from the multi-sig account.
   {% endhint %}


# Analog Testnet Faucet

## Testnet Tokens

ANLOG is the native currency on the Analog Network required to make transactions. See [Tokenomics](/documentation/resources/tokenomics) to learn more. However, to interact with Analog Testnet, you'll need testnet tokens instead of actual ANLOG.&#x20;

{% hint style="warning" %}
Testnet tokens are for experimental use only and hold no monetary value. They enable you to test your app on the testnet before launching on the Analog Mainnet. The faucet is designed to provide small amounts of testnet tokens for your testing purposes and should not be used for activities such as validator staking.
{% endhint %}

Here is a simple, verified 3-step process to acquire the testnet tokens:

1. [Download and set up your wallet](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets).
2. Find the **`#faucet`** channel on the [Analog Discord](https://discord.com/invite/analog).
3. Enter your `!faucet` and your wallet address in the **`#faucet`** channel, and wait for the testnet tokens to be deposited into your account. For example, if your wallet address is `an5CAW5yrSdrbaN35zYCB76NgEmAxEYosFCJ6ie1w79LpEaJnU`, enter it in the faucet channel as follows:

```bash
!faucet an5CAW5yrSdrbaN35zYCB76NgEmAxEYosFCJ6ie1w79LpEaJnU
```

{% hint style="danger" %}
Note that you must enter a valid Analog address, which should always begin with the prefix “`an`.” Check out [Wallets & Accounts ](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts)to learn more.
{% endhint %}


# Staking

Timechain employs Substrate’s Nominated Proof-of-Stake (NPoS) mechanism, a Proof-of-Stake (PoS) variation in which block production is delegated to a set of validators. A key feature of the NPoS protocol is that any token holder can become a nominator, delegate/nominate their stake, and earn a share of the validator rewards.

For more details, check out this [documentation](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/learn-consensus).

{% hint style="success" %}
*Analog staking mechanism provides a new layer of cryptoeconomic security. It allows node operators, investors, and community members to increase security guarantees and user assurances of network services by staking ANLOG tokens, with rewards and penalties incentivizing proper network operation.*
{% endhint %}

This guide covers Analog Network staking: its mechanism, goals, initial implementation details, and future roadmap.

## Key Concepts

* **Direct Staking**: A token holder has the ability to directly nominate their stake to validators and receive direct payments/commissions from them.
* **Nomination Pool (Indirect Staking):** A single account has the ability to stake funds on behalf of a group of users, managing nominations and receiving rewards.
* **Validator**: Secure the network and earn rewards, which are distributed among nomination pools on the Timechain. Active validators can be viewed on the [Timechain Explorer](https://explorer.analog.one/#/validators) or [Polkadot.js Apps Explorer](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/explorer). For more details, see [Timechain Nodes](/documentation/node-operators/introduction/overview#timechain-nodes).
* **Nominator**: An account that selects a validator to stake its ANLOG tokens.  For Analog, this is the nomination pool’s account.
* **Commission**: A share of rewards that validators and nominators earn for their services.
* **Era**: A 12-hour period during which active validators produce blocks and undertake Timechain operations.
* **Session**: A timeframe during which validators are selected as block producers for particular time slots. Each era is split into 4 sessions (i.e., 3 hours).

## Staking Roadmap

| Feature/Timeline  | Phase 1                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Phase 2                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                              |
| ----------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ |
| Staking Approach  | <p></p><ul><li><strong>Default Nomination Pool</strong>: Token holders will be able to stake their ANLOG tokens in a single nomination pool. </li><li><strong>Analog Validators</strong>: The analog staking mechanism will only consist of Analog validators. Whitelisted validators to be added later.</li></ul>                                            | <p></p><ul><li><strong>Permissionless validators</strong>: Any node operator can join the network as a validator, and nominations from any pool or nominator can back them.</li><li><strong>Direct Staking</strong>: Token holders will have the ability to directly nominate their stake to validators of their choice.</li><li><strong>Permissionless Nomination Pools</strong>: Analog will democratize the creation of Nomination Pools by making them permissionless. </li><li><strong>Governance</strong>: A Governance Mechanism will be introduced, allowing token holders to define the initial Nomination Pool configurations (e.g., how many pools users can create, how many members can join a pool, etc.).  </li></ul> |
| Vested Tokens     | Token holders will only be able to stake unlocked/transferrable tokens.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                       | Token holders will be able to stake both unlocked and locked tokens.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                 |
| Staking Dashboard | Analog’s initial staking functionality will be accessible through Substrate-based wallet dashboards, including Talisman and SubWallet. Check out [How to Stake $ANLOG](/documentation/analog-network/staking/how-to-stake-anlog) for more details. The [Polkadot.js extension](https://polkadot.js.org/extension/) will also be supported for advanced users. | For optimal UX, Analog will implement a native, feature-complete, and best-in-class staking dashboard for token holders.                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                                             |


# Network Parameters

Configurable parameters govern the Analog network's operation, including consensus settings (e.g., block time, validator selection) and staking requirements (e.g., bonding, nomination). Understand these parameters, detailed in this document, before staking.

<table data-header-hidden><thead><tr><th width="249" valign="top"></th><th valign="top"></th><th valign="top"></th></tr></thead><tbody><tr><td valign="top"><strong>Parameter</strong></td><td valign="top"><strong>Duration</strong></td><td valign="top"><strong>Description</strong></td></tr><tr><td valign="top">Slot</td><td valign="top">6 seconds (avg)</td><td valign="top">A potential time window for which one block is produced on the Timechain.</td></tr><tr><td valign="top">Epoch</td><td valign="top">3 hours</td><td valign="top">Sequentially indexed periods during which validators are selected to author blocks. </td></tr><tr><td valign="top">Era</td><td valign="top">12 hours</td><td valign="top">A period that  validator sets and their nominators are recalculated and rewards are distributed during a set number of sessions.</td></tr><tr><td valign="top">Nomination period</td><td valign="top">12 hours</td><td valign="top">A period defining how often new validator sets are selected.</td></tr><tr><td valign="top">Bonding duration</td><td valign="top">21 days</td><td valign="top">The time it takes for your staked funds to be transferable after unstaking. Also called <em>unbonding period</em>.</td></tr></tbody></table>


# Rewards

The reward mechanism is based on the NPoS protocol, with some customizations specific to the Analog network. The reward mechanism will be executed through a two-phased methodology:

**Current system:**&#x20;

* Rewards are funded by a 2% pre-allocation from the ***Future Initiatives Pool*** (see [Tokenomics Paper ](https://docs.google.com/document/d/e/2PACX-1vRCpMd7VmDxqGYXYZfj-1RyHdWRU6DfBf1tTad4vlpmmvp4Coavwh9mmT42Te665A/pub)for more details)***.***&#x20;
* Validators earn rewards for block production from the designated pool, while nominators share rewards proportional to their stake.

{% hint style="success" %}
The protocol does not mint new tokens as of now; it distributes rewards from the allocated pool of 2%. The total supply, capped at 9,057,971,000, remains unchanged from the TGE date.
{% endhint %}

**Future inflation model:**&#x20;

* Controlled inflation (1–4%) will replace pre-allocated rewards. See [Tokenomics Paper ](https://docs.google.com/document/d/e/2PACX-1vRCpMd7VmDxqGYXYZfj-1RyHdWRU6DfBf1tTad4vlpmmvp4Coavwh9mmT42Te665A/pub)for more details.
* Inflation parameters (rate, staking thresholds) that are adjustable via governance will largely determine the rewards mechanism. For example,&#x20;
  * Low staking rate → Higher potential APY → Stake more to earn more profit.
  * High staking rate → Lower potential APY → Stake less and use ANLOG elsewhere.
* For more details, read the [Tokenomics Paper](https://docs.google.com/document/d/e/2PACX-1vRCpMd7VmDxqGYXYZfj-1RyHdWRU6DfBf1tTad4vlpmmvp4Coavwh9mmT42Te665A/pub).

## Rewards Calculation

Rewards in Analog are calculated per **era** (12 hours) and distributed to validators based on [**era points**](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/maintain-guides-validator-payout#era-points) earned. Era points are awarded for actions such as:

* Authoring blocks on the Timechain
* Producing references to previously unreferenced blocks

Validators receive rewards proportionally to their earned era points, regardless of their total stake. This approach prevents centralization by ensuring validators with larger stakes don't automatically receive more rewards.

**Example:**\
If the network distributes 1,000 $ANLOG tokens in an era, and there are 4 validators with equal era points:

* Each validator would receive 250 $ANLOG tokens
* These rewards are then shared between the validator and its nominators after the validator commission has been deducted.&#x20;

{% hint style="success" %}
Validators set a commission rate as a percentage of block rewards. This commission is deducted before distributing the remaining rewards to nominators.

**Example:**

* Given a 1,000 $ANLOG token distribution per era, equally split between 4 validators, and validator Alice's 10% commission rate:&#x20;
  * Alice will receive 250 $ANLOG in rewards
  * 25 $ANLOG (10%) goes to Alice as commission
  * The remaining $225 ANLOG is distributed proportionally between Alice's self-stake and her nominators.

For nomination pools, pool operators can also set commissions that apply to rewards before distribution to pool members:

* **Commission Rate**: The percentage of rewards taken by the pool operator
* **Max Commission**: The maximum commission rate the pool can charge
* **Change Rate**: The maximum rate increase allowed for a single commission update

**Example:**

Assuming a pool with a 10% commission receives 1,000 $ANLOG in rewards:

* 100 $ANLOG goes to the pool operator
* 900 $ANLOG is distributed proportionally to pool members based on their contribution
  {% endhint %}

## Claiming Rewards

Due to Substrate's design, validator and nominator rewards in Analog are not automatically distributed and must be claimed via transaction.

### For Direct Nominators (to be implemented later)

1. Rewards can be claimed by calling the `staking.payoutStakers` function
2. Anyone can trigger the payout for all nominators of a validator
3. Rewards must be claimed within 84 eras (approximately 42 days) before they expire

### For Pool Members

Pool members can claim their rewards in two ways:

1. **Manual Claiming**: Navigate to the Pools tab in the staking dashboard and select either:
   * "Withdraw" to add rewards to your transferable balance
   * "Bond" to compound rewards by adding them to your staked amount.&#x20;
2. **Permissionless Claiming**: Enable others to claim rewards on your behalf by setting permissions:

   * `Permissioned`: Only you can claim/bond rewards.
   * `PermissionlessCompound`: Permits others to claim and bond your rewards
   * `PermissionlessWithdraw`: Permits others to claim rewards to your transferable balance
   * <kbd>`PermissionlessAll`</kbd>: Permits both compounding and withdrawing actions by others

   For more details, see [Permissionless Claiming](broken://pages/55OuhHmkvfqrsFoom4Em#permissionless-claiming).


# Boosted Rewards Campaign

The **Boosted Rewards Campaign** is a limited-time incentive program designed to reward early stakers who secure the Analog Network through the [Analog Staking Dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/). By temporarily boosting rewards, this campaign aims to increase network participation and security while offering higher yields to early adopters.

## Campaign Details

### 1. What Is It?&#x20;

* A limited-time event offering additional $ANLOG rewards for staking.
* Participating not only increases your potential yield but also directly contributes to the enhanced security and robustness of the Analog network.

### **2. Campaign Duration**

* The campaign runs for **72 hours** starting from the official announcement.
* Monitor Analog's official social channels for the start time:
  * **X**: <https://x.com/oneanalog?lang=en>
  * **Telegram**: <https://t.me/analogtimer>

### **3. Eligibility**

* **New Stakers:** Stake $ANLOG via the [Analog Staking Dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/) during the 72-hour window.
* **Existing Stakers:** Add more stake through the [dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/) during the campaign period to qualify.

### **4. Reward Pool**

* **Total Allocation:** Up to 3,000,000 $ANLOG.
* **Distribution Phases:**
  * A portion is reserved for early supporters who staked before the campaign.
  * The remainder is distributed proportionally to participants based on stake size and timing.

### **5. Reward Mechanics**

* Eligible stakers will earn additional $ANLOG rewards on top of their standard staking yields.&#x20;
* The amount of boosted rewards earned is proportional to the amount staked and the duration your stake remains bonded within the campaign window.

### 6. Reward Distribution&#x20;

* Boosted rewards are calculated *after* the **72-hour** campaign window concludes.
* Earned boosted rewards will become claimable progressively over a **2-week period**, starting after the 72-hour window ends.

{% hint style="warning" %}
**Unbonding Note:**&#x20;

Users who unbond during the campaign will have rewards calculated later (delays may apply).
{% endhint %}

### **7. How to Track & Claim**

1. Navigate to the **“Positions”** tab on the [Staking Dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/).
2. Click the **“Boosted Rewards”** button to view allocations.
3. Claim rewards daily or wait for full accumulation over 2 weeks.

# Staking Dashboard

The [Staking Dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/staking) provides a streamlined, user-friendly interface for staking ANLOG and managing your configurations.&#x20;

Please note that it is compatible only with specific [supported wallets](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets). Also, note that fund transfers between accounts are not supported through the dashboard, as it is not a wallet. To add funds, use a [supported wallet](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets).

## Overview

Let’s explore the functions of the dashboard.

* **Stake**: Stake ANLOG through a nomination pool.
* **Positions**: View and manage your active staked positions.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FdPrfBHyCVj6ekI600bqd%2FScreenshot%202568-03-31%20at%208.47.13%20PM.png?alt=media&amp;token=c384717d-6be3-48a8-8bc1-7779fc3fe057" alt=""><figcaption><p>Stake Tab</p></figcaption></figure>

* **Additional Details:** Provides a detailed overview of staking info, such as:
  * **Nomination Pool**: The pool where your tokens are bonded.
  * **Unbonding Period:** Time required for staked funds to become transferable after unstaking.
  * **APY**: Estimated annual yield for your stake.
  * **Commission Fee:** Percentage of rewards retained by the pool operator (e.g., 100% = operator keeps all rewards; nominators receive none).
  * **Current Era Ends:** Time remaining until the current staking period (era) ends.

## **How to Stake via the Dashboard**

{% hint style="danger" %}
Ensure your account is funded with enough tokens. This includes the [existential deposit](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#existential-deposit), sufficient free balance for [transaction fees (i.e.inclusion fees)](/documentation/analog-network/staking/faq#what-is-the-minimum-balance-my-account-needs-to-cover-the-inclusion-fee-for-a-transaction-on-the-tim), and the [minimum $ANLOG required to join a nomination pool](/documentation/analog-network/staking/faq#what-is-the-minimum-number-of-usdanlog-tokens-required-to-join-a-nomination-pool-in-analog-staking).&#x20;
{% endhint %}

1. **Connect wallet:** Click "Connect wallet" on the [Staking Dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/).
2. **Enter amount**: Enter the amount of ANLOG you want to stake.
3. **Specify the claiming option:** Specify how you would like your rewards to be claimed:
   1. Auto compound: Your rewards are automatically reinvested into the stake.
   2. Auto withdraw: Your rewards are automatically withdrawn into your wallet.
   3. Manually claim: You'll need to manually claim the rewards.
4. **Confirm the additional details** (nomination pool, unbonding period, APY, etc.) and click "Stake."&#x20;
5. **Approve the transaction** in your wallet.

{% hint style="success" %}
Complete the same steps to add more stake.&#x20;
{% endhint %}

## Unstake ANLOG

1. **Connect wallet:** Click "Connect wallet" on the [Staking Dashboard](https://staking.analog.one/).
2. Navigate to the "Positions" tab and click on "Unstake".
3. **Enter amount**: Enter the amount of ANLOG you would like to unstake.
4. **Approve the transaction** in your wallet.

{% hint style="danger" %}
Unstaked tokens will enter a 21-day unbonding period before withdrawal. It is also worth noting that your account balance must be sufficient to pay the Timechain's inclusion fee and to maintain the minimum [existential deposit](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#existential-deposit).
{% endhint %}


# Third-Party Wallets


# Talisman

## Start Staking <a href="#get-started-with-staking" id="get-started-with-staking"></a>

1. Launch your Talisman wallet extension, click the “Staking” tab, and select the account you want to stake with. Or, you can open the [Talisman Portal](https://app.talisman.xyz/) and click the “Staking” tab on the top-center menu.

{% hint style="info" %}
To use an existing Substrate-based account (such as one from Polkadot.js Extension) in Talisman, you can import it. Refer to this [documentation](https://docs.talisman.xyz/talisman/start/importing-existing-accounts) for more information.
{% endhint %}

2. On the following dashboard, you can select the native asset you’d like to stake, which, in this case, is $ANLOG.

<figure><img src="https://docs.analog.one/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3751219372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%252Fuploads%252Ff9IZ1jUqJGcpUTh15Grr%252FStaking%2520Dashboard.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3Dcea927cf-bd68-4919-966d-9974015947ce&#x26;width=768&#x26;dpr=4&#x26;quality=100&#x26;sign=a98c7c69&#x26;sv=2" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

3. After selecting $ANLOG, you'll be taken to a staking interface where you can choose the account (1), asset (2), token amount to stake (3), and Nomination Pool (4). Upon making your selection, please click the “Stake” button as shown here:

<figure><img src="https://docs.analog.one/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3751219372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%252Fuploads%252FxX9ap2tTOOQpbpTkToKF%252FStake%2520Details.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D156f723f-fe5f-4e46-95d1-b5c25f845c5c&#x26;width=768&#x26;dpr=4&#x26;quality=100&#x26;sign=c81b13a3&#x26;sv=2" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Click “Approve” on the pop-up to confirm your stake.
5. Congratulations! You are now a member of a Nomination Pool and have begun staking. You can now track the status of your staking in the Talisman Portal by navigating to “Positions” on the Staking Dashboard. Here, you can review details such as your nomination pools, balance, recent rewards, and payouts. You’ll also be able to add more stake, unstake, view stats, and modify claim settings. See [Positions Settings](https://docs.analog.one/documentation/analog-network/staking/how-to-stake-usdanlog/talisman#positions-settings) for more details.

### Positions Settings <a href="#positions-settings" id="positions-settings"></a>

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Add More Stake" %}

1. Launch [Talisman Portal ](https://app.talisman.xyz/staking/providers)and navigate to “Staking”->” Positions.”
2. Add assets to your open stake by clicking “Increase Stake” button as shown here:

![](https://docs.analog.one/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3751219372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%252Fuploads%252FznSgsmcoxGk4hDvh4aCL%252FIncrease%2520Stake.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D1e6974ff-c896-4b33-98a1-23e23a48bd9e\&width=768\&dpr=4\&quality=100\&sign=177b3679\&sv=2)

3. Enter the amount of ANLOG you want to increase your stake with and click “Stake.”

![](https://docs.analog.one/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3751219372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%252Fuploads%252FLESlnCmXvcsHsYIyKSy2%252FAmount%2520Staked.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D741e4d96-a059-4a2a-8be2-c7a19c0d30e2\&width=768\&dpr=4\&quality=100\&sign=ab25c56f\&sv=2)

4. Click “Approve” on the pop-up to confirm your transaction.
   {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Unstake" %}

1. Launch [Talisman Portal](https://app.talisman.xyz/staking/providers) and navigate to “Staking”->” Positions.”
2. Click the “three dots beside the "Increase Stake" button and select "Unstake.”

![](https://docs.analog.one/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3751219372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%252Fuploads%252FfiXiZ3AGmwshoKSm0jW4%252FUnstake.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D46f30647-8608-47cb-b956-3be2f5772049\&width=768\&dpr=4\&quality=100\&sign=c80cac07\&sv=2)

Analog requires a 21-day unbonding period before asset withdrawal.

3. Enter the amount of ANLOG tokens to unstake, then click “Unstake” to confirm. Next, click “Approve” on the pop-up to confirm your transaction.

{% hint style="warning" %}
Upon the completion of the unbonding period, you must manually withdraw the funds from the pool so the unstaked token can show in your wallet. When the funds are available, a “withdraw” button will be displayed on the corresponding position row.
{% endhint %}

{% endtab %}

{% tab title="View Stats" %}

1. Launch [Talisman Portal](https://app.talisman.xyz/staking/providers) and navigate to “Staking”-> ”Positions.”
2. Navigate to the “Unstaking” button, click the three dots beside it, and select “Statistics” from the resulting dropdown menu.

![](https://docs.analog.one/~gitbook/image?url=https%3A%2F%2F3751219372-files.gitbook.io%2F%7E%2Ffiles%2Fv0%2Fb%2Fgitbook-x-prod.appspot.com%2Fo%2Fspaces%252FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%252Fuploads%252FggqvwR0yzUZMMCOxfA7I%252FView%2520Stats.png%3Falt%3Dmedia%26token%3D6cb2f324-cf05-4884-85cb-c22830f674ce\&width=768\&dpr=4\&quality=100\&sign=e2540c2a\&sv=2)

3. You should be able to see a detailed breakdown of your current staking position, with details such as the nomination pool you used for this position, the total amount of ANLOG you have staked for this position, staking rewards over 15 days, etc.
   {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Modify Claim Settings" %}

1. Launch [Talisman Portal](https://app.talisman.xyz/staking/providers) and navigate to “Staking”->”Positions.”
2. Navigate to the “Increase Stake” button, click the three dots beside it, and select “Claim settings” from the resulting dropdown menu.
3. Select your preferred Claim Method and choose whether to automatically re-stake your rewards daily (“Auto-compound”) or have them paid to your account daily (“Auto-withdraw”).
   {% endtab %}
   {% endtabs %}


# Polkadot.js Console

## Prerequisites

1\.     Polkadot account. You can [create one](https://support.polkadot.network/support/solutions/articles/65000098878-how-to-create-a-polkadot-account) if you haven’t done so.

## Join a Pool

1. Launch Polkadot.js Console by visiting <https://console.analog.one/#>.
2. Navigate to “Network” -> “Staking”-> “Pools” option.
3. Click the “+Join” button next to the selected pool. In this case, there is only a default pool to join (i.e., `Analog One -Bootstaking`). Note that you can only have an account in one pool at a time.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2F9hJWpfDNgypzVANcZnY7%2FJS%20Console.png?alt=media&amp;token=24d51e9b-407c-4c7e-92b0-44efee07f96f" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Select the account you want to use for nomination and enter the amount of ANLOG to contribute to the pool. Next, click “Join” as shown here:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FLrkrPealoBhQkKdx6PVa%2FJoin%20Nomination%20Pool.png?alt=media&amp;token=62970a2d-b504-4eff-909c-1fed59da2a5f" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Sign and submit the transaction.

{% hint style="danger" %}
After paying transaction fees, if your account balance is less than the [Existential Deposit](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#existential-deposit), your account will be reaped, and you will lose any remaining funds.
{% endhint %}

## Exit a Pool

1. Launch Polkadot.js Console by visiting <https://console.analog.one/#>.
2. Navigate to “Network” -> “Staking”-> “Accounts” ->“Pooled” option.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FyXKAl1P5PkSr93UhmSeB%2FExit%20Pool%201.png?alt=media&amp;token=0acdea30-3add-457b-9693-5a4f673d5bcd" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

3. Select the account from which you want to unbond funds, click the three dots next to the pool, and then click "+Unbond funds."

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FHnDgz7AJVLMqefvrEsuf%2FExit%20Pool%202.png?alt=media&amp;token=83ed04cc-26cc-49a3-b7a1-840e8ed666f2" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Sign and submit the transaction.

{% hint style="danger" %}
Pool withdrawals are subject to a 21-day unbonding period.
{% endhint %}

## Claim Rewards

1. Launch Polkadot.js Console by visiting <https://console.analog.one/#>.
2. Navigate to “Network” -> “Staking”-> “Accounts” ->“Pooled” option.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FH8PS9S3CDU2YFwBy693d%2FExit%20Pool%201.png?alt=media&amp;token=ab38c028-5437-47fc-bf32-ad35aa7d1059" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

3. Click the three vertical dots for the selected pool, then "Withdraw claimable."

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FlqLQTgiXjmztDnuOtpwM%2FWithdraw%20Claimable.png?alt=media&amp;token=994f6f3f-722c-4aa1-a1c0-7c5456c86fb3" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Sign and submit the transaction.

## Withdraw Unbonded Funds

1. Launch Polkadot.js Console by visiting <https://console.analog.one/#>.
2. Navigate to “Network” -> “Staking”-> “Accounts” ->“Pooled” option.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FH8PS9S3CDU2YFwBy693d%2FExit%20Pool%201.png?alt=media&amp;token=ab38c028-5437-47fc-bf32-ad35aa7d1059" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

3. Select the account you want to withdraw funds from and click the three vertical dots for the selected pool, then "Withdraw unbonded."

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FEsLu4edYYcy9BlWQCmra%2FWithdraw%20Unbonded.png?alt=media&amp;token=213a5621-9365-442f-9f91-b96c3ce7aa60" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Sign and submit the transaction.

{% hint style="danger" %}
Like when you nominate manually, your funds will be locked until the unbonding period is over. If you withdraw completely, you'll leave the pool and can then join a different one.
{% endhint %}


# SubWallet

## Start Staking

1. Open the SubWallet extension and select **Earning** (bottom tab).

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FZiZPOqlpxmkk3cTw1v14%2FSubWallet%201.png?alt=media&amp;token=55f47851-8118-4b0e-9944-9ef31f0f721b" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

2. Choose **ANLOG** from the list (use the search bar if needed).

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FKxHv92OjKaAA8kOTwTD1%2FSubWallet%202.png?alt=media&amp;token=9476be0d-acd1-434d-b1d7-cf717b850ab7" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

{% hint style="warning" %}
If you’ve staked ANLOG before, click the **+** icon on the “Your earnings position” screen to access staking options.
{% endhint %}

3. Enter the ANLOG amount to stake, and ensure "Analog One-Bootstaking" pool is selected.
4. Review staking details (i.e., Account Info, Nomination info, etc.), then click **+Stake.**

{% hint style="warning" %}
Check pool details (click the three-dot icon) and ensure your balance exceeds the minimum staking amount.
{% endhint %}

5. Confirm details on the Add to Stake Confirm screen and click **Approve**.

## Positions

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Add More Stake" %}

1. Navigate to **Earning** > **Your Earning Positions** and select your stake.
2. Click **Claim** **Rewards** > **Continue**.
3. Confirm details and click **Approve**.
   {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Claim Rewards" %}

1. Navigate to **Earning** > **Your Earning Positions** and select your stake.Click **Claim Rewards** > **Continue**.
2. Confirm details and click **Approve**.
   {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Unstake" %}

1. Go to **Earning** > **Your Earning Positions** and select your stake.
2. Click **+Unstake**, enter the amount, then click -Unstake.
3. Confirm details and click **Approve**.
   {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Withdraw Unstaked Tokens" %}
{% hint style="warning" %}
Tokens must complete the unbonding period before withdrawal.
{% endhint %}

1. **Track Withdrawal Time:**
   1. Go to **Earning** > **Your Earning Positions** > select unstaked funds.
   2. Check the countdown under **Withdraw Info**.
2. **Withdraw Tokens:**
   1. After the unbonding period, return to **Withdraw Info** and click **Withdraw** > **Continue**.
   2. Confirm details and click **Approve**.
      {% endtab %}
      {% endtabs %}


# FAQ

Find answers to frequently asked questions about Analog Staking in the section below. For questions not listed, we invite you to ask them on our [Discord](https://discord.com/invite/analog)!

## General Staking Questions

<details>

<summary><strong>What is Analog Staking?</strong></summary>

Staking on Analog/Timechain allows you to earn rewards while contributing to network security by locking up your $ANLOG tokens. It uses Substrate's Nominated Proof-of-Stake (NPoS) mechanism where token holders can nominate their stake to validators who produce blocks and secure the network.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How does Analog’s staking differ from Polkadot’s?</strong></summary>

Analog's NPoS mechanism is built on Substrate, the same framework used by Polkadot, so they share core functionality. However, Analog has customized certain parameters like era duration (12 hours vs. 24 hours on Polkadot) and may implement unique features in the future. See [Network Parameters ](/documentation/analog-network/staking/network-parameters)for more details.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What are the benefits of staking $ANLOG?</strong></summary>

Staking $ANLOG provides multiple benefits including earning passive income through rewards, contributing to network security, and participating in the Analog ecosystem. Early stakers can enjoy higher returns, with APY potentially reaching around 45% when 40% of tokens are staked.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Who are Validators and Nominators?</strong></summary>

* **Validators:** Secure the network, produce blocks, and earn rewards. They must maintain high uptime and integrity.
* **Nominators:** ANLOG holders who delegate tokens to validators or pools. Nominators share rewards but assume risks like slashing if their validator misbehaves.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is a Nomination Pool?</strong></summary>

A Nomination Pool is a type of staking pool in the Substrate ecosystem that allows users to combine their tokens to stake with multiple validators. This enables participation in staking without needing a large amount of tokens. The pool is managed by the pool operator.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How are validators selected?</strong></summary>

Currently, validator access is restricted to a whitelisted group, but the network will transition to an open system where anyone can participate. Validator selection will then be determined by stake backing (self-stake and nominations) and performance. Active validators can be viewed on the [Timechain Explorer ](https://explorer.analog.one/#/validators)or [Polkadot.js Apps.](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/staking/targets)

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Can I stake both unlocked and locked tokens?</strong></summary>

Currently, token holders can only stake unlocked/transferrable tokens. However, staking both unlocked and locked tokens will be supported in the future.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Can I use the same tools for Analog staking as I do for Polkadot?</strong></summary>

Yes, many Substrate ecosystem tools, such as Polkadot.js, Talisman, and SubWallet, support Analog staking. However, for the best experience, Analog will provide a dedicated staking dashboard.

</details>

## Rewards & Economics

<details>

<summary><strong>What is the minimum number of $ANLOG tokens required to join a nomination pool in Analog Staking?</strong></summary>

The minimum number of tokens required to join a nomination pool on the Analog Network is **1 $ANLOG**. However, this requirement may change with future network updates or parameters..

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is the estimated APY for staking?</strong></summary>

APY depends on the staking rate:

* **Low staking rate**: Higher APY (incentivizes participation).
* **High staking rate**: Lower APY.\
  Initial rewards come from 2% of the token supply; later, 8% annual inflation will fund rewards and the Treasury.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How are rewards calculated?</strong></summary>

Rewards are distributed per 12-hour **era**. Validators earn points for actions like block production, and rewards are split proportionally among nominators.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>When are rewards paid out?</strong></summary>

Rewards are distributed after each era. You must claim them manually via your wallet or dashboard. Unclaimed rewards expire after 84 eras (\~42 days).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is the long-term inflation model?</strong></summary>

After initial emissions, an **8% annual inflation rate** will fund validator/nominator rewards and the Treasury. Governance can adjust this rate.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Will there be more nomination pools?</strong></summary>

Yes. Analog plans to enable **permissionless nomination pools**, allowing users to create and join pools freely.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Will Analog implement on-chain governance for staking parameters?</strong></summary>

Yes, Analog plans to introduce a governance mechanism allowing token holders to vote on nomination pool configurations and other staking parameters, giving the community direct input on the staking ecosystem.

</details>

## Technical

<details>

<summary><strong>What is the minimum balance my account needs to cover the inclusion fee for a transaction on the Timechain, and how is this fee calculated to ensure my transaction won’t be rejected?</strong></summary>

The inclusion fee is the minimum required fee for a transaction to be processed on the Timechain. It consists of:

* **`Base Fee:`** A fixed amount applied to all transactions, set by the runtime.&#x20;
* **`Length Fee`**: A charge calculated by multiplying the transaction size (in bytes) by a set rate.
* **`Weight Fee`**: A charge based on the computational resources your transaction consumes

The formula is:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
Inclusion Fee = Base Fee + Length Fee + Weight Fee
```

{% endcode %}

Additionally, you might want to ensure that:

* A 1 $ANLOG minimum deposit is required to activate a wallet. See [Existential Deposit](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#existential-deposit) for more details.
* Transaction fees typically range from `0.001-0.005 $ANLOG`, varying with network usage and transaction complexity.
* Fees are deducted before your transaction is executed.
* Transactions will fail if your balance cannot cover both the inclusion fee and maintain the minimum required [Existential Deposit](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#existential-deposit).

</details>

<details>

<summary>W<strong>hat does "existential deposit" mean in the context of the Analog Network, and why is it required?</strong></summary>

The existential deposit is the minimum amount of tokens an account must hold to remain active on the Analog Network. If an account’s balance falls below this threshold, the network may remove (or “reap”) the account to optimize storage. The existential deposit is necessary to:

* **Prevent state bloat:** Accounts with very small balances or empty accounts are labeled as “dust accounts.” Without an existential deposit, these accounts could accumulate in large numbers, leading to unnecessary data storage and processing demands on the blockchain.
* **Maintain network performance:** By enforcing a minimum balance, the network avoids being slowed down by excessive inactive or irrelevant accounts, ensuring faster transaction processing and lower fees for active users.
* **Enhance security and scalability:** The existential deposit helps prevent malicious actors from creating millions of low-balance accounts to spam or overload the network.

For more details, refer to [Existential Deposit](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#existential-deposit).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is an Era, Session, and Bonding Duration?</strong></summary>

* **Era**: 12 hours (reward distribution cycle).
* **Session**: 3 hours (validator rotation window).
* **Bonding Duration**: 21 days (unbonding period after unstaking).

For more details, see [Network Parameters](/documentation/analog-network/staking/network-parameters).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How do I stake $ANLOG?</strong></summary>

Currently, use [**Talisman**](broken://pages/JyfBOjXkhRg3Y2HvZVxs), [**Polkadot.js**](broken://pages/55OuhHmkvfqrsFoom4Em)**,** or **SubWallet**. A native Analog Staking Dashboard is under development for improved UX.

</details>

## Local Development Network

This section requires using the local development network set up with Docker as described [here](/documentation/node-operators/infrastructure-setup/running-a-full-node-on-the-mainnet).
