# Developers' Guide

Start building next-gen cross-chain DApps with Analog GMP.


# Getting Started

This quick start guide outlines how to connect your contract with Analog Gateway contracts to enable cross-chain messaging. On the origin chain, the source contract sends encoded data via the [`submitMessage`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#submitmessage) function. The payload is then routed to the destination chain, where the destination contract receives and processes it using the [`onGmpReceived`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#igmpreceiver-interface) function.

## Prerequisites

1. Basic knowledge of Solidity.
2. Network IDs for source and destination chains. See [Gateway Addresses & Network IDs](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids) for more details.&#x20;
3. An EVM wallet set up and funded with enough tokens for the chains you’ll be working with. For testnet tokens, use the following faucet links:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Ethereum Sepolia" %}

* [Alchemy Faucet](https://www.alchemy.com/faucets/ethereum-sepolia)
* [PoW Faucet](https://sepolia-faucet.pk910.de/)
  {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Astar Shibuya" %}

* [Astar Portal](https://portal.astar.network/shibuya-testnet/assets) (Follow the steps [here](https://docs.astar.network/docs/build/environment/faucet/))
  {% endtab %}

{% tab title="BNB Smart Chain" %}

* [BNB Faucet](https://www.bnbchain.org/en/testnet-faucet)
* [BNB Discord](https://discord.com/channels/789402563035660308/1099937267021250560)
* [QuickNode](https://faucet.quicknode.com/binance-smart-chain/bnb-testnet)
* [Chainstack](https://faucet.chainstack.com/bnb-testnet-faucet)
  {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Arbitrum Sepolia" %}

* [Alchemy](https://www.alchemy.com/faucets/arbitrum-sepolia)
* [Chainlink](https://faucets.chain.link/arbitrum-sepolia)
* [QuickNode](https://faucet.quicknode.com/arbitrum/sepolia)
* [Getblock](https://getblock.io/faucet/arb-sepolia/)
  {% endtab %}
  {% endtabs %}

## Procedure

Follow these steps to send a GMP request from chain A to chain B.&#x20;

<details>

<summary><strong>Step 1: Implement Receiver Contract</strong></summary>

All destination contracts must implement [`IGmpReceiver`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#igmpreceiver-interface):

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.0;

import {IGmpReceiver} from "./IGmpReceiver.sol";

contract CrossChainReceiver is IGmpReceiver {
    // Store the Gateway address for security checks
    address public immutable gateway;
    
    // Events to track received messages
    event MessageReceived(bytes32 id, uint128 sourceNetwork, bytes32 source, bytes payload);
    
    constructor(address _gateway) {
        gateway = _gateway;
    }
    
    function onGmpReceived(
        bytes32 id,
        uint128 sourceNetwork,
        bytes32 source,
        uint64 nonce,
        bytes calldata payload
    ) external payable override returns (bytes32) {
        // Security check: only the Gateway can call this function
        require(msg.sender == gateway, "Unauthorized sender");
        
        // Process the received message
        emit MessageReceived(id, sourceNetwork, source, payload);
        
        // Implement your cross-chain logic here
        // ...
        
        // Return a result (can be used for verification or additional logic)
        return keccak256(abi.encodePacked(id, block.timestamp));
    }
}

```

{% endcode %}

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Step 2: Send Cross-Chain Messages</strong></summary>

Use the [Gateway](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#igateway-interface) to send messages between chains:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.0;

import {IGateway} from "./IGateway.sol";

contract CrossChainSender {
    // Gateway contract reference
    IGateway public immutable gateway;
    
    // Events to track sent messages
    event MessageSent(bytes32 messageId, address destination, uint16 destinationNetwork);
    
    constructor(address _gateway) {
        gateway = IGateway(_gateway);
    }
    
    function sendCrossChainMessage(
        address destinationContract,
        uint16 destinationNetwork,
        bytes calldata payload
    ) external payable returns (bytes32) {
        // Estimate the cost of sending the message
        uint256 gasCost = gateway.estimateMessageCost(
            destinationNetwork,
            payload.length,
            100000 // Gas limit for execution on destination chain
        );
        
        // Ensure enough funds are provided
        require(msg.value >= gasCost, "Insufficient funds for cross-chain message");
        
        // Send the message
        bytes32 messageId = gateway.submitMessage{value: msg.value}(
            destinationContract,
            destinationNetwork,
            100000, // Gas limit
            payload
        );
        
        emit MessageSent(messageId, destinationContract, destinationNetwork);
        return messageId;
    }
}
```

{% endcode %}

**Gas Management:**

* When sending messages, always ensure you provide enough gas for execution on the destination chain.  Use estimateMessageCost to estimate costs:&#x20;

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
gateway.estimateMessageCost(networkId, messageSize, gasLimit)
```

{% endcode %}

* Use `destinationNetwork` from Gateway's `networkId()`

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Step 3: Make a GMP request</strong></summary>

* Deploy your source and destination chain contracts using the gateway addresses for the specified networks. You can find these addresses on the [Gateway Addresses & Network ID ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)page.&#x20;
* Estimate the gas cost by calling `estimateMessageCost` on the gateway by passing in the appropriate [`networkid`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids), `messageSize`, and `gasLimit`.
* Make the GMP call using the `submitMessage` method. Here's an example:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
gateway.submitMessage{value: msg.value}(dest_address, dest_network, GMP_GAS_LIMIT, payload);
```

{% endcode %}

When making the GMP call using the `submitMessage` method, ensure that the gas fee is included as a value in the request. If the value provided is less than the estimated message cost, the transaction will fail with the error: "`execution reverted: insufficient tx value.`"

* Upon finalizing the transaction on the source chain, a `Task ID` will be created on the Timechain. You can track the status of your task on the [Timechain Explorer](https://explorer.testnet.analog.one/#/gmp). For more details, see [Track GMP Messages](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/track-gmp-messages).&#x20;
* Once your task is completed, your destination chain contract state will be updated.

Congratulations! You just sent your first GMP request using Analog GMP. Next, try [tracking the status of the GMP call](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/track-gmp-messages) after you send it.

</details>

## Next Steps

Try [tracking the status of the GMP call](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/track-gmp-messages) after you send it.

Build your first[ cross-chain DApp using Analog GMP](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/tutorials/send-a-message-from-chain-a-to-chain-b).

See how [Foundry can help you set up your cross-chain project](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/tutorials/setup-your-project-with-foundry).


# Track GMP Messages

This section explains how to track GMP messages from Source Chain → Timechain → Destination Chain.

## GMP Message Flow & Key Identifiers

1. **Message Initiation (Source Chain):**
   1. A user or dApp calls the [**`submitMessage`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#submitmessage) function on the Gateway contract on the source chain.
   2. This action emits a [**`GmpCreated`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpcreated) event.
   3. **Key takeaway**: This event contains the crucial **`Message ID`** (&#x61;**`bytes32`**&#x68;ash) and the **Source Transaction Hash**.
2. **Timechain Processing:**
   1. Timechain detects the [**`GmpCreated`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpcreated) event after it's finalized on the source chain and creates internal tasks to manage this message. These tasks have **`Task IDs`**.
   2. Messages progress through the following statuses as they're processed:
      * **`CREATED`**: The message has been logged on the source chain by the Explorer's listeners.
      * **`MESSAGE_RECEIVED`**: Timechain has officially acknowledged receiving the message.
      * **`PROCESSING`**:   Timechain is preparing the message for secure delivery to the destination chain.
3. **Message Execution (Destination Chain):**
   1. A Timechain node (relayer) calls the [**`execute`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#execute) or [**`batchExecute`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#batchexecute) function on the Gateway contract on the destination chain.
   2. This action emits a [**`GmpExecuted`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) event, which includes the same **`Message ID`** and the execution result.
   3. **Key takeaway:** The transaction that executes the message on the destination chain is the Destination Transaction Hash.
4. **Confirmation (Timechain & Explorer):**
   * **`MESSAGE_EXECUTED`:** The [**`GmpExecuted`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) event has been logged by the Explorer on the destination chain.&#x20;
   * **`COMPLETED/FAILED:`** Timechain confirms the final outcome of the message on the destination chain.&#x20;

## Timechain Explorer

The [**Timechain Explorer**](https://explorer.analog.one/) is your main tool for monitoring GMP messages as they move from a source chain, through the Timechain, to the destination chain. See [**Timechain Explorer**](/documentation/resources/tooling/timechain-explorer#gmp) for more details.&#x20;

## How to Track Your GMP Message

Let's say you've just sent a GMP message using an Analog GMP-powered dApp.

**Step 1: Get Information from the Source Chain**

* **Get the Source Transaction Hash:** After initiating a GMP message, your wallet or the dApp will provide a **transaction hash** for the action on the source blockchain.
* **Access the Source Chain's Block Explorer:** Go to the block explorer for the source chain. For example, use [**Arbiscan**](https://arbiscan.io/) for Arbitrum or [**Etherscan**](https://sepolia.etherscan.io/) for Ethereum Sepolia.
* **Find Your Transaction:** Use the **Source Transaction Hash** to locate your transaction on the block explorer.
* **Locate the** [**`GmpCreated`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpcreated) **Event:** In the transaction details, look for the "**Logs**" or "**Events**" section. Find the [`GmpCreated`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpcreated) event, which is emitted by the Gateway contract.
* **Extract the `messageId`:** Within the [**`GmpCreated`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpcreated) event details, you'll find the **`messageId`**. This is a `bytes32` value, typically displayed as a hex string. Make sure to note it down. You can also record other details, like `destinationAddress` and `destinationNetwork` for confirmation.

**Step 2: Use the Timechain Explorer**

* **Access GMP Tab:** Open the [Timechain Explorer ](https://explorer.analog.one/)and navigate to the "GMP" tab.
* **Search by Message ID:** In the search bar, paste the **`messageId`** you obtained from the source chain explorer.
* **View Message Details:** The explorer will display the current status and detailed information of your GMP message.
* **Interpreting the Status on Timechain Explorer:**
  * **`CREATED`**: Message submitted on source chain.
  * **`MESSAGE_RECEIVED`**: Timechain has received the message
  * **`PROCESSING`**: Timechain is preparing the message for submission to the destination chain.
  * **`MESSAGE_EXECUTED`**: The [**`GmpExecuted`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) event has been logged by the Explorer on the destination chain.
  * **`COMPLETED`**: Message successfully verified and executed on the destination chain, confirmed by the Timechain.
  * **`FAILED`**: Message execution failed on the destination chain, or a critical error occurred during Timechain processing.

{% hint style="success" %}
The **Destination Transaction Hash** should still be available to investigate the failure on the destination chain explorer. Other Searchable IDs include:

* **`Task ID:`** If you have a specific Timechain Task ID related to your GMP message, you can also use this to search. Timechain Explorer links **`Task IDs`** to **`Message IDs`**.
* **`Batch ID:`** If your message was processed as part of a batch by the Timechain, searching by **`Batch ID`** will show all messages in that batch.
  {% endhint %}


# Troubleshooting Failed Messages

Failures can occur at various stages. Here’s how to troubleshoot:

## Failure: Transaction Reverted on Source Chain

**Symptom**: Your attempt to call [**`submitMessage()`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#submitmessage) on the source Gateway contract fails immediately or reverts.

**Diagnosis**:

* Check the transaction on the source chain's explorer.
* Look for the revert reason provided by the Gateway contract. Common reasons include:
  * **`insufficient tx value`**: You haven't sent enough native currency to cover the estimated gas costs for Timechain processing and destination execution.
  * **`msg data too large`**: Your message payload exceeds `MAX_PAYLOAD_SIZE`.
  * **`invalid gmp network`**: The destination network ID is not supported or misconfigured.

**Remedy**:

* Adjust the `msg.value` sent with [`submitMessage`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#submitmessage) based on [`estimateMessageCost`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/fee-management#estimatemessagecost)
* Reduce the size of your message data.
* Verify the destination network ID is correct and supported.

## Failure: Message Submitted but Not Processed by Timechain

**Symptom**: [**`GmpCreated`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpcreated) event is visible on the source chain, but the `Message ID` doesn't appear on the Timechain Explorer after a reasonable period, or its status remains `CREATED` for too long.

**Diagnosis**:

* **Confirm Source Chain Finality**: Ensure your source chain transaction is deeply finalized.&#x20;
* **Check Chronicle Node Status**: The "Chronicles" pane on the Explorer can give clues.
* **Explorer Data Lag**: The Explorer itself might have a slight delay in indexing.

**Remedy**:

* Wait for sufficient finality on the source chain.
* If consistently delayed, check [Analog's Discord ](https://discord.com/channels/860069399627825163/1199286156777496656)for reported issues with Chronicle nodes or the specific source chain integration.

## Failure: Message Stuck or Failed During Timechain Processing

**Symptom**: Message appears on Timechain Explorer, but status is stuck (e.g., `MESSAGE_RECEIVED`, `PROCESSING`).&#x20;

**Diagnosis**:

* **Timechain Explorer**:
  * Look for linked **Task IDs** in the message details.
  * Search for these Task IDs on the Explorer.
* **Shard Status**: Check the "Shards" pane on the Explorer. If the relevant shard is offline, message processing might be stalled.

**Remedy**:

* If a shard is offline, the system is designed for fault tolerance; however, significant shard outages could still cause delays. Check [Analog's Discord ](https://discord.com/channels/860069399627825163/1199286156777496656)for reported issues.

## Failure: Execution Failed on Destination Chain

**Symptom**: Timechain Explorer shows the message status as `MESSAGE_EXECUTED` but indicates a failure (or `FAILED`), or the [**`GmpExecuted`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) event on the destination chain shows `status = REVERT`.

**Diagnosis**:

* **Destination Gateway** [**`GmpExecuted`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) **Event**: This is the most direct source of information.
  * `id`: Matches your GMP `Message ID`.
  * `status`: Will be `REVERT` (or equivalent enum for failure).
  * `result`: May contain an error message or a hash of the error data from the execution attempt.
* **Destination Gateway Contract Logs/Trace**:
  * Check the transaction hash on the destination chain's explorer.
  * Examine internal calls or revert reasons from the Gateway's `_execute` function. Common issues:
    * **`insufficient gas to execute GMP message`**: The `executionGasLimit` set in the original [**`submitMessage`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#submitmessage) was too low for the actual execution on the destination chain (including the Gateway's overhead and the call to your [**`onGmpReceived`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#ongmpreceived) function).
    * **Target Contract Revert**: Your `I`[**`GmpReceiver::OnGmpReceived`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#ongmpreceived) function (or any contract it calls) reverted. The `result` field from [**`GmpExecuted`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) or the transaction trace on the destination explorer should provide the specific revert reason from your contract.

**Remedy**:

* **Insufficient Gas**: Resubmit the message from the source chain with a higher `executionGasLimit`.
* **Target Contract Revert**: Debug your destination contract's [**`OnGmpReceived`**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#ongmpreceived) logic. Ensure it can handle the incoming data format, has enough gas internally, and doesn't violate any conditions. The `result` from [`GmpExecuted`](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#gmpexecuted) is key here.


# Tutorials

This section offers detailed, step-by-step guides to help you become proficient in building on Analog GMP.


# Send a Message from Chain A to Chain B

This guide provides you with an overview of the `Counter` smart contract, its functionality, and how it can be used as a building block for cross-chain dApps. The contract leverages a gateway mechanism to facilitate cross-chain communication.

## Overview

The `Counter` contract is a simple example of a cross-chain-enabled smart contract. It listens for messages sent via a gateway and increments a counter (`number`) whenever a valid message is received. This functionality can be extended to build more complex cross-chain dApps. You can find this contract [here](https://github.com/Analog-Labs/analog-gmp-examples/blob/main/examples/simple/Counter.sol).

<details>

<summary>Code Overview</summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.0;

import {IGmpReceiver} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGmpReceiver.sol";

contract Counter is IGmpReceiver {
    address private immutable _gateway;
    uint256 public number;

    constructor(address gateway) {
        _gateway = gateway;
    }

    function onGmpReceived(bytes32, uint128, bytes32, bytes calldata) external payable returns (bytes32) {
        require(msg.sender == _gateway, "unauthorized");
        number++;
        return bytes32(number);
    }
}
```

{% endcode %}

### Features

1. **Cross-chain message processing**: The contract implements the `IGmpReceiver` interface, which defines the `onGmpReceived` function for handling incoming cross-chain messages.
2. **State management**: Maintains a counter (`number`) that increments upon receiving valid messages.
3. **Gateway authentication**: Ensures only authorized messages from the specified gateway are processed.

</details>

### Contract Structure

### 1. **Imports**

The contract imports the `IGmpReceiver` interface:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
import {IGmpReceiver} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGmpReceiver.sol";
```

{% endcode %}

This interface defines the structure for handling cross-chain messages.

### 2. **State Variables**

* `_gateway`: Stores the address of the gateway responsible for relaying cross-chain messages.
* `number`: A public variable that tracks the current count.

### 3. **Constructor**

The constructor initializes the `_gateway` variable with the address of the gateway:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
constructor(address gateway) {
    _gateway = gateway;
}
```

{% endcode %}

### 4. **onGmpReceived Function**

This function is triggered when a cross-chain message is received. It performs the following actions:

1. Verifies that the sender is the authorized gateway.
2. Increments the `number` state variable.
3. Returns the updated counter value as a `bytes32`.

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
function onGmpReceived(bytes32, uint128, bytes32, bytes calldata) external payable returns (bytes32) {
    require(msg.sender == _gateway, "unauthorized");
    number++;
    return bytes32(number);
}
```

{% endcode %}

## Cross-Chain Communication Flow

### **1. Initiate Transaction**

* Send a message from the source chain through the Gateway:

<pre class="language-solidity"><code class="lang-solidity"><strong>// Example sender contract
</strong>function sendCrossChainMessage(address targetContract) external payable {
    gateway.sendMessage{value: msg.value}(
        targetContract,
        DESTINATION_CHAIN_ID,
        ""
    );
}
</code></pre>

### **2. Message Processing**

* Gateway validates and routes the message
* Destination gateway calls **`onGmpReceived`**

### **3. State Update**

* **`counter`** increments on successful message receipt
* Return value sent back as execution receipt

## Example Use Case: Cross-Chain Counter Increment

Imagine you want to track an event occurring across multiple chains (e.g., user actions). You can use this contract as follows:

1. Deploy it on each chain with the same gateway address.
2. Configure your gateway to relay events from one chain to another.
3. Use the incremented counter (`number`) as a measure of cross-chain activity.

### Prerequisites

To get started with Analog GMP, you need to have the following components ready:

1. IDE, such as [Remix IDE](https://remix.ethereum.org/#lang=en\&optimize=false\&runs=200\&evmVersion=null\&version=soljson-v0.8.25+commit.b61c2a91.js)
2. EVM wallet, such as [Talisman](https://www.talisman.xyz/), [MetaMask](https://metamask.io/), etc.
3. Acquire enough faucet tokens. Here are some links to access the faucets:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Sepolia" %}

* [Alchemy Faucet](https://www.alchemy.com/faucets/ethereum-sepolia)
* [PoW Faucet](https://sepolia-faucet.pk910.de/)
  {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Shibuya" %}

* [Astar Portal](https://portal.astar.network/shibuya-testnet/assets) (Follow the steps [here](https://docs.astar.network/docs/build/environment/faucet/))
  {% endtab %}

{% tab title="Polygon Amoy" %}

* [Alchemy](https://www.alchemy.com/faucets/polygon-amoy)
* [Polygon Faucet](https://faucet.polygon.technology/)
  {% endtab %}
  {% endtabs %}

#### Deploy the Counter.sol Contract on the Destination Chain

Deploy the `Counter.sol` contract on the `Sepolia` network:

1. [Open the Counter.sol ](https://remix.ethereum.org/#url=https://github.com/Analog-Labs/analog-gmp-examples/blob/main/examples/simple/Counter.sol\&optimize=false\&runs=200\&evmVersion=null\&version=soljson-v0.8.0+commit.e5eed63a.js\&lang=en)in Remix.&#x20;
2. Compile the contract.
3. Now, deploy the contract as follows:

* Open your MetaMask wallet and switch to the “**Sepolia**” network.
* While still in Remix IDE, locate the “**Deploy & Run Transactions"** tab and select “***Injected Provider – MetaMask***” in the **Environment** list.
* Fill in the amount of ETH token you’d like to pay as gas fees in the “Gas Limit” text and the gateway address in the “***`At Address”`*** text. You can find a list of gateway addresses [here](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids).&#x20;
* Now click on the “***Deploy***” button to deploy your contract. MetaMask will prompt you to confirm the transaction. Check to ensure that you’re deploying the contract on the Sepolia testnet and confirm the transaction.

{% hint style="info" %}
Please take note of the address of the contract you just deployed. You will need it when submitting a cross-chain request on the source chain. You can find this address under the "***Deployed /Unpinned Contracts***" list or on the [Sepolia Explorer](https://sepolia.etherscan.io/).&#x20;
{% endhint %}

#### Setup the IGateway Interface on the Source Chain

To initiate a cross-chain call on the destination chain, interact directly with the **`IGateway`** interface on the **`Shibuya`** network (the source chain). No contract deployment on Shibuya is required for this process:

1. While still in Remix IDE, open your MetaMask wallet and switch from `Sepolia` to `Shibuya` network.
2. Locate the “***Contracts***” tab in Remix IDE and select “**`IGateway`**” contract. &#x20;
3. Navigate to the “***At Address***” text and enter the gateway address for `Shibuya`. You can find gateway addresses [here](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids). You should now be able to see the `IGateway` contract displayed under the “***Deployed /Unpinned Contracts***” tab when you click on the “***At Address***” text.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FycU4ePMUdlLCR3Xw6VF6%2FDeployed%20Gateway%20Contract.png?alt=media&amp;token=4d224398-a5af-47fd-a594-e8956a7e9c42" alt=""><figcaption><p>IGateway Contract</p></figcaption></figure>

#### Fund the Destination Gateway

Cross-chain calls require the sender to fund the destination gateway with sufficient gas for execution:

1. Under the “***Deployed /Unpinned Contracts***” tab, locate the `IGateway` contract and expand on it. You should be able to see four functions as shown here:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FzG6wgPnY9syAdrAgkd56%2FIGateway%20Functions.png?alt=media&amp;token=ed5322ad-2bec-4104-8dd9-9b7d378a8ba8" alt=""><figcaption><p>IGateway Functions</p></figcaption></figure>

2. Navigate to the **`deposit`** function and fill in the details about the `sender` and the `sourceNetwork`.  See [*Address Conversion Details* ](#address-conversion-details)to learn more about address conversions. Also, refer to [*Gateway Addresses & Network IDs*](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids) for more details about `sourceNetwork`. In this case, the sender can be your account or a smart contract address, and the `sourceNetwork` is `7` for `Shibuya`.
3. Next, enter the amount of `ETH` tokens you’d like to pay as gas fees in the “**value**” text, and click the “**Transact**” button. This value should be enough to cover the gas cost of the message you will send from the source chain.
4. Click transact and sign the transaction on your Wallet. You can now verify the amount of funds available on the destination gateway by specifying the sender address and destination network ID in the **`depositOf`** function.

When funding the destination's chain Gateway, you can use an [*Externally Owned Account (EOA)* or a *contract address*](https://ethereum.org/en/developers/docs/accounts/). For Ethereum addresses that are **20 bytes** long, you must convert them into a **32-byte address**. This conversion is necessary because Analog GMP is designed to enable dApps to interact with any asset or application on any connected chain, extending beyond just EVM compatibility.&#x20;

You can learn more about the conversion details in the subsequent section:

<details>

<summary>Address Conversion Details</summary>

To convert an EOA hex address into bytes32, simply pad the address with zeros(0s) from the left as follows:

`0x000....000`

Note that the last padded digit is `0`. For example, "`0x818F34269759Bf180709e7eBbaeF94b2651058b3`" will convert to “`0x000000000000000000000000818F34269759Bf180709e7eBbaeF94b2651058b3`."&#x20;

To convert a contract hex address into 32bytes, pad the address with zeros (0s) and ensure that the last padded digit is one (1) as follows:

`0x000...01`.&#x20;

For example, "`0x818F34269759Bf180709e7eBbaeF94b2651058b3`" will convert to “`0x000000000000000000000001818F34269759Bf180709e7eBbaeF94b2651058b3`." Notice that the last padded digit is one (`1`).

Alternatively, you could also use this contract to convert a `20-byte` address into `bytes32`.&#x20;

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: GPL-3.0
pragma solidity >=0.7.0 <0.9.0;

contract ConvertAddress {
    function toGmpAddress(address addr, bool isContract) public pure returns (bytes32 source) {
        uint256 contractFlag = isContract ? 1 << 160 : 0;
        source = bytes32(contractFlag) | bytes32(uint256(uint160(addr)));
    }

    function toGmpAddress(address addr) external view returns (bytes32 source) {
        bool isContract = addr.code.length > 0;
        source = toGmpAddress(addr, isContract);
    }
}
```

{% endcode %}

</details>

#### Dispatch a Cross-Chain Message to the Destination Chain

Dispatch a cross-chain message from Shibuya to the contract you deployed on Ethereum Sepolia:

1. Open MetaMask and select the **`Shibuya`** testnet.
2. Under the “***Deployed/Unpinned Contracts***” tab, locate the `IGateway` contract and expand on it. Expand the **`submitMessage`** function and fill in the following arguments:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FvHyqQD4kSmYsZuRlVSRs%2FSend%20Cross-chain%20Message.png?alt=media&amp;token=480766ae-6e1d-45cd-b23d-e9209b343348" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

* `destinationAddress`: The destination contract address. Enter the deployed contract address on the Sepolia network here.
* `destinationNetwork`: Analog GMP’s identifier for the destination chain. You can find these details [here](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids). For example, in the case of the Sepolia network, the `destinationNetwork` should be 5.
* `executionGasLimit`: Gas limit available for the contract call, e.g., `100000`.
* `data`: Any `string`, e.g., `0x`.

3. Next, click the “**transact**” button to run the method and confirm the transaction in your MetaMask.

{% hint style="warning" %}
Note that the transaction might fail if the gas prices spike significantly. If this happens, fund your destination gateway with more gas and try the transaction again. If the transaction goes through, you can note its log details, as shown [here](https://shibuya.blockscout.com/tx/0xe562fb36d627cee434d187af350f81f3b86e01e70a579a16d898a2e610729dc5?tab=logs) on Shibuya Explorer.
{% endhint %}


# Teleport Tokens from Chain A to Chain B

This tutorial shows how to move ERC-20 tokens from Astar Shibuya to Ethereum Sepolia. The full source code for the contract utilized in this demo is available [here](https://github.com/Analog-Labs/analog-gmp-examples/blob/main/examples/teleport-tokens/BasicERC20.sol).&#x20;

## Overview

### Token Teleportation

The contract allows tokens to be "teleported" (transferred) between chains through these functions:

1. **`teleport(address recipient, uint256 amount)`**:
   * Burns tokens from the sender on the source chain
   * Creates a cross-chain message with the transfer details
   * Submits the message to the gateway contract
   * Returns a unique **`message ID`**
2. **`onGmpReceived(bytes32 id, uint128 network, bytes32 sender, bytes calldata data)`**:
   * Receives the cross-chain message on the destination chain
   * Validates the message source
   * Mints tokens to the recipient on the destination chain
3. **`teleportCost(uint16 networkid, address recipient, uint256 amount)`**:
   * Estimates the cost (in native currency) required to teleport tokens

## **Cross-Chain Workflow**

### **On the Source Chain**:

* User calls `teleport`, burning tokens and initiating a cross-chain message.
* The gateway relays the message to the destination chain.

### **On the Destination Chain**:

* The gateway triggers `onGmpReceived` on the target `BasicERC20` contract.
* Tokens are minted to the recipient.

## Step-by-Step Guide

<details>

<summary>Step 1: Instantiating the contract</summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT

pragma solidity >=0.8.0;

import {ERC20} from "@solmate/tokens/ERC20.sol";
import {IGmpReceiver} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGmpReceiver.sol";
import {IGateway} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGateway.sol";
import {GmpSender, PrimitiveUtils} from "@analog-gmp/Primitives.sol";
contract BasicERC20 is ERC20, IGmpReceiver {

}
```

{% endcode %}

1. Import `ERC20.sol` from "@solmate/tokens.
2. Import `IGmpReceiver.sol` and `IGateway.sol` from `@analog-gmp/interfaces`.
3. Import  `GmpSender` and `PrimitiveUtils` from `@analog-gmp`.
4. Inherit the `IGmpReceiver.sol` contract into your main contract (i.e., `BasicERC20`).

</details>

<details>

<summary>Step 2: Create state variables and constructor</summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
IGateway private immutable _trustedGateway;
    BasicERC20 private immutable _recipientErc20;
    uint16 private immutable _recipientNetwork;

    /**
     * @dev Emitted when `amount` tokens are teleported from one account (`from`) in this chain to
     * another (`to`) in another chain.
     *
     * Note Is not necessary to emit the destination network, because this is already emitted by the gateway in `GmpCreated` event.
     */
    event OutboundTransfer(bytes32 indexed id, address indexed from, address indexed to, uint256 amount);

    /**
     * @dev @dev Emitted when `amount` tokens are teleported from one account (`from`) in another chain to
     * an account (`to`) in this chain.
     *
     * Note Is not necessary to emit the source network, because this is already emitted by the gateway in `GmpExecuted` event.
     */
    event InboundTransfer(bytes32 indexed id, address indexed from, address indexed to, uint256 amount);

    /**
     * @dev Gas limit used to execute the `onGmpReceived` method.
     */
    uint256 private constant MSG_GAS_LIMIT = 100_000;

    /**
     * @dev Command that will be encoded in the `data` field on the `onGmpReceived` method.
     */
    struct TeleportCommand {
        address from;
        address to;
        uint256 amount;
    }

    constructor(
        string memory name,
        string memory symbol,
        IGateway gatewayAddress,
        BasicERC20 recipient,
        uint16 recipientNetwork,
        address holder,
        uint256 initialSupply
    ) ERC20(name, symbol, 10) {
        _trustedGateway = gatewayAddress;
        _recipientErc20 = recipient;
        _recipientNetwork = recipientNetwork;
        if (initialSupply > 0) {
            _mint(holder, initialSupply);
        }
    }

```

{% endcode %}

1. Create an instance of the `_trustedGateway` of type `IGateway`. This is the contract that will submit the message to the target chain. Next, create two instances:  `_recipientErc20` of type `BasicERC20` and `_recipientNetwork` of type `uint16`.
2. Create two events: `OutboundTransfer` that will be emitted when tokens are teleported from Shibuya to Sepolia, and `InboundTransfer` will be emitted when the tokens are teleported from one account (\``from`\`) in another chain to an account (\``to`\`) in this chain.
3. Create a `TeleportCommand` of type struct that will be encoded in the “`data`” field of the “`onGmpReceived`” function.
4. Create the constructor with the parameters: `name`, `symbol`, `gatewayAddress`, `recipient`, `recipientNetwork`, and `holder`, and set them appropriately. For example, this contract needs a reference to the `IGateway` and `ERC20`,  so we’ll pass these details on to the constructor.

</details>

<details>

<summary>Step 3: Teleport the tokens</summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
/**
     * @dev Teleport tokens from `msg.sender` to `recipient` in `_recipientNetwork`
     */
    function teleport(address recipient, uint256 amount) external returns (bytes32 messageID) {
        _burn(msg.sender, amount);
        bytes memory message = abi.encode(TeleportCommand({from: msg.sender, to: recipient, amount: amount}));
        messageID = _trustedGateway.submitMessage(address(_recipientErc20), _recipientNetwork, MSG_GAS_LIMIT, message);
        emit OutboundTransfer(messageID, msg.sender, recipient, amount);
    }
```

{% endcode %}

1. Create a function named **`teleport`** that will send the tokens from Shibuya to Sepolia. This function has the following parameters:

* `recipient`: the receiver of ERC-20 tokens on the destination chain.
* `amount`: the amount of ERC-20 tokens the recipient receives.

2. Burn the ERC-20 tokens specified by the `amount` variable for a given sender on the source chain.
3. Create a GMP payload (i.e., `message`). For our `BasicERC20` DApp, the `message` should contain the sender, recipient, and amount. We’ll need to abi-encode these parameters and set them as the `message`.
4. Call the Gateway contract to generate a cross-chain request. Call the `submitMessage` method of the Gateway contract with the required parameters to route the message to the destination chain. For more details about this function, read [here](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components#submitmessage).

</details>

<details>

<summary>Step 4: Handling the GMP call</summary>

After setting up the logic for teleporting tokens from the source chain, the next step is to implement the **`onGmpReceived`** method of the `IGmpReceiver` interface to manage the GMP request on the target chain. The `onGmpReceived` function will consist of the following signature:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
function onGmpReceived(bytes32 id, uint128 network, bytes32 sender, bytes calldata data)
        external
        payable
        returns (bytes32)
    {
        // Convert bytes32 to address
        address senderAddr = GmpSender.wrap(sender).toAddress();

        // Validate the message
        require(msg.sender == address(_trustedGateway), "Unauthorized: only the gateway can call this method");
        require(network == _recipientNetwork, "Unauthorized network");
        require(senderAddr == address(_recipientErc20), "Unauthorized sender");

        // Decode the command
        TeleportCommand memory command = abi.decode(data, (TeleportCommand));

        // Mint the tokens to the destination account
        _mint(command.to, command.amount);
        emit InboundTransfer(id, command.from, command.to, command.amount);

        return id;
    }

```

{% endcode %}

1. It’s important that this function's name remains **`onGmpReceived`** and that its signature, including all the parameters, remains the same. This is crucial as the Gateway contract on the destination chain will call this function. Any modifications to the name or parameters could lead to a failed call.
2. Ensure that only the Gateway contract calls this function; no other addresses or contracts should have access to it.
3. Ensure that the network and sender address are validated.
4. Decode the packet using ABI encoding the data received and `TeleportCommand` and store it in the `command` variable. &#x20;
5. Mint the tokens to the destination account and emit the `InboundTransfer` event with the `id`, `command.from`, `command.to`, and `command.amount`.

</details>


# Setup Your Project with Foundry

This tutorial will show you how to install and use Analog GMP contracts with Foundry.

## Getting Started

1. Ensure you’ve [installed Foundry](https://book.getfoundry.sh/getting-started/installation).
2. To start a new project with Foundry, use `forge init`:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
forge init hello_gmp
```

{% endcode %}

This creates a new directory called “`hello_gmp`” from the default Foundry template and initializes a new GIT repository.

3. You may have to add your `remappings` in `remappings.txt` by running this command:&#x20;

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
forge remappings > remappings.txt
```

{% endcode %}

4. Your project should now be correctly set up to work with Analog GMP contracts. From now on, simply import the Analog GMP dependencies into your contract from `@analog-gmp` as follows:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
import {IGmpReceiver} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGmpReceiver.sol";
import {IGateway} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGateway.sol";
```

{% endcode %}

For example, create a sample contract in the src directory, such as `counter.sol`, using the following code:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.0;

import {IGmpReceiver} from "@analog-gmp/interfaces/IGmpReceiver.sol";

contract Counter is IGmpReceiver {
    address private immutable _gateway;
    uint256 public number;

    constructor(address gateway) {
        _gateway = gateway;
    }

    function onGmpReceived(bytes32, uint128, bytes32, bytes calldata) external payable returns (bytes32) {
        require(msg.sender == _gateway, "unauthorized");
        number++;
        return bytes32(number);
    }
}

```

{% endcode %}

5. Now run:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
forge build
```

{% endcode %}

This should display a successful compilation message, as shown here:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
[⠊] Compiling...
[⠊] Compiling 28 files with Solc 0.8.13
[⠒] Solc 0.8.13 finished in 949.34ms
Compiler run successful!
--> src/Counter.sol
```

{% endcode %}

## Writing Tests

You can easily write cross-chain unit tests using Analog  testing tools at `@analog-gmp-testing`.

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
import {GmpTestTools} from "@analog-gmp-testing/GmpTestTools.sol";

// Deploy gateway contracts and create forks for all supported networks
GmpTestTools.setup();

// Set `account` balance in all networks
GmpTestTools.deal(address(account), 100 ether);

// Switch to Sepolia network
GmpTestTools.switchNetwork(5);

// Switch to Shibuya network and set `account` as `msg.sender` and `tx.origin`
GmpTestTools.switchNetwork(7, address(account));

// Relay all pending GMP messages.
GmpTestTools.relayMessages();
```

{% endcode %}

### Build

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
forge build
```

{% endcode %}

### Test

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
forge test 
```

{% endcode %}

This should display a successful test message, as shown here:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
[⠊] Compiling...
No files changed, compilation skipped

Ran 2 tests for test/Counter.t.sol:CounterTest
[PASS] testFuzz_SetNumber(uint256) (runs: 256, μ: 30977, ~: 31288)
[PASS] test_Increment() (gas: 31303)
Suite result: ok. 2 passed; 0 failed; 0 skipped; finished in 19.97ms (9.69ms CPU time)

Ran 1 test suite in 145.98ms (19.97ms CPU time): 2 tests passed, 0 failed, 0 skipped (2 total tests)
```

{% endcode %}


# FAQ

Here are some frequently asked questions we receive, along with their answers. If you have a question that we haven’t answered, feel free to ask it on our [Discord](https://discord.com/invite/analog) channel.&#x20;

## Fundamentals

<details>

<summary><strong>What is Analog GMP?</strong></summary>

Analog GMP is a composable, permissionless, on-chain toolkit that enables messages to flow freely and securely across supported blockchains. It facilitates [cross-chain smart contract execution calls](https://www.analog.one/blog/introduction-to-cross-chain-smart-contract-execution-calls), value transfer, and message delivery, allowing developers to build cross-chain DApps that can leverage liquidity from multiple blockchains.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Is Analog GMP a blockchain?</strong></summary>

No. Analog GMP is not a blockchain even though it is built on top of a sovereign blockchain, the Timechain. Timechain creates an accountability layer for all transactions occurring across Analog GMP. Some of the protocol events that Timechain manages include registration and tracking of validators, management of shards through key rotations, and initiation of tasks on the destination shards to trigger execution of cross-chain requests.

For more details, check out [Timechain](broken://pages/FFKdDmWRjwfiMsKkAYjb).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is $ANLOG?</strong></summary>

ANLOG is the native token for the Analog Network. ANLOG token holders contribute to decentralized security and governance and earn rewards and fees.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What are the benefits of using Analog GMP for blockchain developers?</strong></summary>

Analog GMP benefits both blockchain and DApp developers. Blockchain developers can tap into user bases, assets, and valuable states from supported blockchains using Analog GMP. DApp developers, on the other hand, can create cross-chain network effects and liquidity, allowing users to access their DApps from preferred chains effortlessly with Analog GMP.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Is Analog GMP a cross-chain bridge?</strong></summary>

While you could build specific cross-chain bridges on top of Analog GMP, the platform isn’t your typical cross-chain bridge. At its core, Analog GMP is a general message-passing platform that leverages a sovereign blockchain (i.e., Timechain) to pass messages securely and reliably across supported blockchains. As such, it transcends the idea of transferring tokens from one chain to another. For example, with Analog GMP, you can move arbitrary messages (data), value (token), and message + value (i.e., programmable tokens) between different connected chains.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How can I get the testnet ANLOG tokens?</strong></summary>

You can request Testnet ANLOG (TANLOG) tokens in the Faucet channel in our Discord. Check out [**Testnet Faucet** ](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/analog-testnet-faucet)for more details.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What role do TSS shards play in security?</strong></summary>

* **Threshold Signatures:** Shards are groups of nodes that collaboratively sign messages using Schnorr signatures, ensuring no single point of failure.
* **Management:** Admins can register (`setShard`) or revoke (`revokeShard`) shard keys. Active shards are stored in `ShardStore`.
* **Verification:** Signatures are validated using stored public keys, and revoked shards cannot sign new messages.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is the purpose of the Gateway contract?</strong></summary>

The Gateway contract facilitates cross-chain communication by enabling the submission and execution of GMP messages. It allows users to send messages to different blockchain networks, manages security through Threshold Signature Scheme (TSS) shards, and ensures message integrity via cryptographic signatures.

</details>

## Protocol

<details>

<summary><strong>How secure is Analog GMP?</strong></summary>

Analog GMP is built on top of the Timechain, a battle-tested NPoS consensus protocol, to secure cross-chain transactions. Timechain ensures message validity through a permissionless ecosystem consisting of dynamic validator sets and employs Threshold Cryptography to connect to external blockchains, requiring supermajority approval for transaction execution. See the [Security & Audits page](/documentation/analog-network/security-and-audits) for more details.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How does the Gateway prevent replay attacks?</strong></summary>

* **Replay Protection:** The contract uses a mapping `_executedMessages` to store hashes of executed messages, preventing reuse.
* **Nonce Tracking:** Each sender has a nonce (`nonceOf`) that increments with every message, ensuring uniqueness.
* **Signature Verification:** Messages require valid Schnorr signatures from authorized TSS shards, tied to specific shard IDs.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How are cross-chain messages authenticated?</strong></summary>

* **Source Validation:** Messages include a `source` field (address or contract flag) to identify the origin.
* **Signature Checks:** Inbound messages require valid Schnorr signatures from the current active shard, verified via `_verifySignature`.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What is the time to finality for transactions occurring across Analog GMP?</strong></summary>

The time to finality depends on three factors: the time it takes for the transaction to be considered final on the source chain, the time it takes for the Timechain to create the task for the destination shard (which is minimal), and the time it takes for the transaction to be executed on the destination chain. &#x20;

Before signing any attestation for a source chain event, the designated source chain shard must wait for a pre-determined number of on-chain block confirmations to pass to ensure the transaction is final. This is essential for the integrity and security of the transaction.

For more details, see "[Time to Finality](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/time-to-finality)."

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How are gas fees calculated for cross-chain messages?</strong></summary>

* **Estimation:** The `estimateMessageCost` function computes fees based on the target network, message size, and gas limit.
* **Dynamic Costs:** Fees account for network-specific parameters (e.g., gas price, message size) stored in `RouteStore`.
* **Payment:** Users must attach sufficient funds when calling `submitMessage`, which is used to cover execution costs on the destination chain.

&#x20;Refer to the [Fee Management](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/fee-management) page for more details.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How are batch operations optimized for gas efficiency?</strong></summary>

* **Command Lookup Table:** Batched commands (e.g., GMP, shard updates) use a lookup table (`_cmdTableLookup`) for constant-time dispatch, minimizing gas costs.
* **Memory Reuse:** Memory is recycled during batch execution to prevent exponential expansion costs.
* **Gas Refunds:** Excess gas is refunded to the caller after execution, incentivizing efficient batch submissions.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What would happen if sender sends <code>submitMessage</code> with insufficient gas fees?</strong></summary>

If the user calls the `submitMessage` and provides a low `gasLimit`, the GMP message will be delivered just fine. However, during execution, the message might return with an `Out of Gas` error, causing the user to be charged regardless.&#x20;

On the other hand, if the user sets the gas limit to `100000`, for example, but pays less than the `relativeGasPrice*100000` when calling `submitMessage`, the gateway will reject the message and return an “`insufficient tx value error`,” reimbursing the user in the process.

</details>

<details>

<summary> <strong>What is <code>messageSize</code> in the <code>estimateMessageCost</code> function?</strong></summary>

It is the size of the payload in bytes.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What happens when I submit a message on Analog GMP?</strong></summary>

A dApp/contract on the origin chain calls `submitMessage()` on its gateway contract. The origin chain's shard relays this message to the Timechain upon detecting an `GmpCreated` event and completing TSS operations. The Timechain then creates a GMP message for the destination shard, which executes it by calling `execute()` after validation. Refer to [submitMessage()](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components) and [execute()](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/architectural-components) methods.&#x20;

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Can I send large payloads via GMP?</strong></summary>

* **Size Limit:** Messages are capped at `MAX_PAYLOAD_SIZE` (defined in `Primitives.sol`). Exceeding this limit reverts the transaction.
* **Cost Impact:** Larger payloads increase gas fees, as estimated by `estimateMessageCost`.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>What happens if a GMP message fails to execute?</strong></summary>

* **Status Tracking:** Each message’s status (`GmpInfo`) is stored, with states like `SUCCESS`, `REVERT`, or `PENDING`.
* **Retries:** Failed messages (status `REVERT`) must be resubmitted with a new nonce. The contract does not auto-retry.
* **Event Logging:** The `GmpExecuted` event records the result, including error data for debugging.<br>

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How do I track the status of a GMP message?</strong></summary>

* **Query Function:** Use `gmpInfo(bytes32 id)` to check the status (`GmpStatus`) and block number of a message.
* **Events:** The `GmpCreated` and `GmpExecuted` events emit message IDs, source/destination details, and execution results.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>How long does it take for a message to be detected on the Timechain after submission?</strong></summary>

After finalization on the source chain (which varies by blockchain, see [Time to Finality](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/overview/time-to-finality) for more details), messages typically appear on the Timechain within 1-2 blocks.

</details>


# Building With OATS

**Unlock true token mobility**. The **Omni-Chain Analog Token Standard (OATS)** streamlines cross-chain transfers within the Analog ecosystem, offering a standardized and user-friendly experience built on the power of [Analog GMP.](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp)

To get started with OATS and make your token omni-chain, follow the instructions in the [Quickstart Guides ](#quickstart-links)below.

***

## Why Build with OATS?

**OATS** provides a seamless and secure framework to make your tokens universally available. Move beyond complex bridging and fragmented liquidity with a single, powerful solution.

**Core Features:**

* **Native Cross-Chain Transfers:** Your tokens move seamlessly across any Analog-connected network without the need for wrapping or fragmented liquidity pools.
* **Simplified Deployment:** Integrate cross-chain capabilities with minimal effort using standardized interfaces.&#x20;
* **Robust Security:** All transfers are secured by the [Timechain](/documentation/developers/protocol-overview#the-timechain-analogs-core), which leverages [Threshold Signature Schemes (TSS) and Multi-Party Computation (MPC)](/documentation/developers/protocol-overview#chronicle-nodes-and-shards-enabling-cross-chain-communication) to eliminate single points of failure.
* **Enhanced Capital Efficiency:** By avoiding wrapped assets and fragmented liquidity, OATS fosters a unified and highly efficient liquidity environment for your token.
* **Frictionless UX:** Provide your token holders with an intuitive and secure method for transferring their assets across their preferred chains.

***

# Build Your First Omni-Chain dApp

This guide explains how to deploy OATS tokens and bridge them between Ethereum Sepolia and Arbitrum Sepolia testnets via **`burn/mint`** or **`lock/unlock`**.

***

## **Prerequisites**

* [MetaMask](https://metamask.io/en-GB) browser extension installed.
* **Testnet ETH** on both Ethereum Sepolia and Arbitrum Sepolia. You can get this from [public faucets](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/getting-started#prerequisites).

***

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Burn/Mint" %}

## **Step 1: Get the OATS Token Code**

1. Open Remix IDE: [https://remix.ethereum.org/](https://www.google.com/search?q%3C1%3E=https://remix.ethereum.org/)
2. Create a new file named `OATSToken.sol`.
3. Copy and paste the following code into the file. This is a simplified version of the  `OATSToken` contract for demonstration.

<details>

<summary><strong><code>OATSToken.sol</code></strong></summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.0;

import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/ERC20.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/token/ERC20/extensions/ERC20Burnable.sol";
import "@openzeppelin/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";

// A simplified interface for the Analog Gateway
interface IGateway {
    function submitMessage(address destinationAddress, uint16 network, uint64 gasLimit, bytes calldata data) external payable returns (bytes32);
    function estimateMessageCost(uint16 network, uint16 messageSize, uint64 gasLimit) external view returns (uint256);
}

// A simplified interface for receiving messages
interface IGmpReceiver {
    function onGmpReceived(bytes32 id, uint128 networkId, bytes32 source, uint64 nonce, bytes calldata data) external payable returns (bytes32);
}

contract OATSToken is ERC20, ERC20Burnable, Ownable, IGmpReceiver {
    IGateway public immutable gateway;
    mapping(uint16 => address) public supportedTokens;

    constructor(string memory name, string memory symbol, address _gateway) ERC20(name, symbol) Ownable(msg.sender) {
        gateway = IGateway(_gateway);
        _mint(msg.sender, 1000000 * 10**decimals());
    }

    function setSupportedToken(uint16 networkId, address tokenAddress) public onlyOwner {
        supportedTokens[networkId] = tokenAddress;
    }

    function estimateSendCost(uint16 destinationNetworkId) public view returns (uint256) {
        bytes memory message = abi.encode(msg.sender, msg.sender, 0);
        return gateway.estimateMessageCost(destinationNetworkId, uint16(message.length), 100000);
    }

    function send(uint16 destinationNetworkId, address recipient, uint256 amount) public payable {
        require(supportedTokens[destinationNetworkId] != address(0), "Unsupported network");
        _burn(msg.sender, amount);
        bytes memory message = abi.encode(msg.sender, recipient, amount);
        gateway.submitMessage{value: msg.value}(supportedTokens[destinationNetworkId], destinationNetworkId, 100000, message);
    }

    function onGmpReceived(bytes32, uint128, bytes32, uint64, bytes calldata data) external payable override returns (bytes32) {
        // In a real scenario, you'd verify the gateway and source.
        (, address to, uint256 amount) = abi.decode(data, (address, address, uint256));
        _mint(to, amount);
        return bytes32(0);
    }
}

```

{% endcode %}

Here are the main functions you'll interact with as a user:

**`balanceOf(address account)`**&#x52;eturns the number of OATS tokens owned by a specific account. This is a standard ERC20 function.

* **Parameters:**
  * `account`: The address you want to query the balance of.
* **Returns:** The token balance of the account.

**`transfer(address recipient, uint256 amount)`**&#x54;ransfers a specified amount of OATS tokens to another address on the **same** network. This is a standard ERC20 function.

* **Parameters:**
  * `recipient`: The address of the person receiving the tokens.
  * `amount`: The number of tokens to send.

**`estimateSendCost(uint16 destinationNetworkId)`** Calculates the estimated cost in the native currency (e.g., ETH) to send tokens to another network.&#x20;

* **Parameters:**
  * `destinationNetworkId`: The unique identifier of the network you want to send tokens to.
* **Returns:** The estimated fee for the transaction.

**`send(uint16 destinationNetworkId, address recipient, uint256 amount)`**&#x53;ends a specified amount of OATS tokens to a recipient on a **different** supported network. This function will burn your tokens on the current network and trigger the minting of tokens on the destination network.

* **Parameters:**
  * `destinationNetworkId`: The unique identifier of the network you are sending tokens to.
  * `recipient`: The address of the recipient on the destination network.
  * `amount`: The number of tokens you want to send.

{% hint style="warning" %}
When calling the **`send`** function, you must also send a payment in the native currency (e.g., ETH) that is at least equal to the value returned by `estimateSendCost`. This payment covers the transaction fees on the destination network.
{% endhint %}

</details>

***

## **Step 2: Deploy on Ethereum Sepolia**

1. **Compile:** In Remix, go to the "**Solidity Compiler**" tab, select a compiler version `^0.8.0`, and click "**Compile OATSToken.sol**".
2. **Deploy:**
   * Go to the "**Deploy & Run Transactions**" tab.
   * Select "**Injected Provider - MetaMask**" as the environment.
   * Make sure your MetaMask is connected to the "**Ethereum Sepolia**" network.
   * In the "**Deploy**" section, next to the "Deploy" button, provide the constructor arguments:
     * `name`: "MyOATSToken"
     * `symbol`: "MOT"
     * `_gateway`: (Use the Sepolia Analog GMP Gateway address). See [Gateway Addresses & Network IDs ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)for more details.&#x20;
   * Click "**Deploy**" and confirm the transaction in MetaMask.
3. **Copy the Deployed Address:** Once deployed, copy the address of your token contract on Sepolia.

***

## **Step 3: Deploy on Arbitrum Sepolia**

1. **Switch Network:** In MetaMask, switch to the "**Arbitrum Sepolia**" network.
2. **Deploy Again:** Repeat the deployment steps from Step 2, but this time connected to Arbitrum Sepolia. Use the same constructor arguments, but for  `_gateway` use the Arbitrum address. See [Gateway Addresses & Network IDs ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)for more details.&#x20;
3. **Copy the Deployed Address:** Copy the address of your token contract on Arbitrum Sepolia.

***

## **Step 4: Configure the Tokens**

1. **On Ethereum Sepolia:**
   * In Remix, at the deployed contract address for your Sepolia token, call the `setSupportedToken` function.
   * `networkId`: (The chain ID for Arbitrum Sepolia). See [Gateway Addresses & Network IDs ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)for more details.&#x20;
   * `tokenAddress`: The address of the token you deployed on Arbitrum Sepolia.
   * Click "**transact**" and confirm in MetaMask.
2. **On Arbitrum Sepolia:**
   * Switch your MetaMask to Arbitrum Sepolia.
   * In Remix, at the deployed contract address for your Arbitrum token, call `setSupportedToken`.
   * `networkId`: (The chain ID for Ethereum Sepolia). See [Gateway Addresses & Network IDs ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)for more details.&#x20;
   * `tokenAddress`: The address of the token you deployed on Ethereum Sepolia.
   * Click "**transact**" and confirm.

***

## **Step 5: Transfer Tokens**

1. **Switch to Sepolia:** In MetaMask, switch back to the Ethereum Sepolia network.
2. **Estimate Cost:** In Remix, for your Sepolia token contract, call the `estimateSendCost` function with the Arbitrum Sepolia `networkId`. This will give you the required fee in wei.
3. **Send Tokens:**
   * Call the `send` function.
   * `destinationNetworkId`: The Arbitrum Sepolia `networkId`.
   * `recipient`: The address you want to receive the tokens on Arbitrum.
   * `amount`: The amount of tokens to send (in wei, e.g., `1000000000000000000` for 1 token).
   * In the "**Value**" field at the top of the Remix panel, enter the cost estimated in the previous step, in wei.
   * Click "**transact**" and confirm in MetaMask.

Your newly minted tokens will arrive at your Arbitrum Sepolia wallet address in a few minutes. You can confirm the transaction and view your updated balance on [Arbiscan](https://sepolia.arbiscan.io/)
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Lock/Unlock" %}

## **Objective**

To bridge a custom ERC20 token (**`MyToken`**) from Ethereum Sepolia to Arbitrum Sepolia. We'll lock tokens in a bridge on Ethereum and unlock them from a pre-funded bridge on Arbitrum.

***

## **Step 1: Gather Contract Code and Key Addresses**

First, we need to set up our smart contracts in Remix and note down the essential Analog protocol addresses. See [**Gateway Addresses & Network IDs** ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)for more details. Proceed as follows:

1. Open the [Remix IDE.](https://remix.ethereum.org/#lang=en\&optimize=false\&runs=200\&evmVersion=null)
2. In the `File Explorers` tab, create the following 3 files and paste the code into them.

**1. `MyToken.sol`** This is the custom ERC-20 token we will be bridging.

<details>

<summary><strong><code>MyToken.sol</code></strong></summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

import "https://github.com/OpenZeppelin/openzeppelin-contracts/blob/v4.9.3/contracts/token/ERC20/ERC20.sol";

// A standard, pre-existing ERC20 token.
contract MyToken is ERC20 {
    constructor() ERC20("My Test Token", "MTT") {
        _mint(msg.sender, 1000000 * 10**18); // Mint 1 Million tokens to the deployer
    }
}
```

{% endcode %}

</details>

**2. `Interfaces.sol`** This file will contain all the necessary interfaces:

<details>

<summary><strong><code>Interfaces.sol</code></strong></summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

// --- GMP Interfaces ---
interface IGateway {
    function estimateMessageCost(uint16 network, uint16 messageSize, uint64 gasLimit) external view returns (uint256);
    function submitMessage(address destinationAddress, uint16 network, uint64 gasLimit, bytes calldata data) external payable returns (bytes32);
}

interface IGmpReceiver {
    function onGmpReceived(bytes32 id, uint128 networkId, bytes32 source, uint64 gasLimit, bytes calldata data) external payable returns (bytes32);
}

// --- OATS Interfaces ---
library Utils {
    error UnauthorizedGW(address caller);
    error UnknownNetwork(uint16 networkId);
}

interface ISender {
    function cost(uint16 networkId) external view returns (uint256);
    function send(uint16 networkId, address recipient, uint256 amount) external payable returns (bytes32 msgId);
}
```

{% endcode %}

m

</details>

**3. `OATSBridge.sol`** This is the main bridge contract that implements the **`lock/unlock`** logic.

<details>

<summary><strong><code>OATSBridge.sol</code></strong></summary>

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```solidity
/// SPDX-License-Identifier: MIT
pragma solidity ^0.8.19;

import "https://github.com/OpenZeppelin/openzeppelin-contracts/blob/v4.9.3/contracts/token/ERC20/IERC20.sol";
import "https://github.com/OpenZeppelin/openzeppelin-contracts/blob/v4.9.3/contracts/access/Ownable.sol";
import "./Interfaces.sol";

/// @title OATSBridge (Lock/Unlock)
/// @notice An OATS-compliant bridge for an existing ERC20 token.
contract OATSBridge is ISender, IGmpReceiver, Ownable {
    IGateway private immutable _gateway;
    IERC20 private immutable _token;

    mapping(uint16 => address) public supportedNetworks;

    uint64 private constant _GAS_LIMIT = 100_000;

    struct TransferPayload {
        address recipient;
        uint256 amount;
    }

    event TokensLocked(address indexed from, uint16 indexed toNetwork, address indexed toRecipient, uint256 amount);
    event TokensUnlocked(address indexed to, uint16 indexed fromNetwork, uint256 amount);

    constructor(address gatewayAddress, address tokenAddress) Ownable() {
        _gateway = IGateway(gatewayAddress);
        _token = IERC20(tokenAddress);
    }

    function setNetwork(uint16 networkId, address bridgeAddress) public onlyOwner {
        supportedNetworks[networkId] = bridgeAddress;
    }

    function cost(uint16 networkId) external view returns (uint256) {
        bytes memory message = abi.encode(TransferPayload({recipient: address(0), amount: 0}));
        return _gateway.estimateMessageCost(networkId, uint16(message.length), _GAS_LIMIT);
    }

    function send(uint16 networkId, address recipient, uint256 amount) external payable returns (bytes32) {
        address targetBridge = supportedNetworks[networkId];
        if (targetBridge == address(0)) {
            revert Utils.UnknownNetwork(networkId);
        }

        // 1. Lock tokens: Pull tokens from the user into this bridge contract.
        _token.transferFrom(msg.sender, address(this), amount);

        bytes memory message = abi.encode(TransferPayload({recipient: recipient, amount: amount}));

        emit TokensLocked(msg.sender, networkId, recipient, amount);
        return _gateway.submitMessage{value: msg.value}(targetBridge, networkId, _GAS_LIMIT, message);
    }

    function onGmpReceived(bytes32, uint128 networkId, bytes32 source, uint64, bytes calldata data)
        external
        payable
        returns (bytes32)
    {
        if (msg.sender != address(_gateway)) {
            revert Utils.UnauthorizedGW(msg.sender);
        }
        address sourceBridge = address(uint160(uint256(source)));
        if (supportedNetworks[uint16(networkId)] != sourceBridge) {
            revert Utils.UnknownNetwork(uint16(networkId));
        }

        TransferPayload memory payload = abi.decode(data, (TransferPayload));

        // 1. Unlock tokens: Transfer tokens from the bridge to the recipient.
        _token.transfer(payload.recipient, payload.amount);

        emit TokensUnlocked(payload.recipient, uint16(networkId), payload.amount);
        return keccak256("OATS_UNLOCK_SUCCESS");
    }
}
```

{% endcode %}

</details>

***

## Step 2: Compile Contracts

1. Navigate to the **Solidity Compiler** tab in Remix.
2. Select a compiler version compatible with the code (e.g., `0.8.19`).
3. Compile `MyToken.sol` and `OATSBridge.sol`.&#x20;

***

## Step 3: Deploy on Ethereum Sepolia

1. In the `Deploy & Run Transactions` tab, set the `ENVIRONMENT` to **Injected Provider - MetaMask**.
2. Ensure your MetaMask is connected and set to the **Ethereum Sepolia** network.
3. **Deploy `MyToken.sol`:**
   * Select `MyToken - contracts/MyToken.sol` from the `CONTRACT` dropdown.
   * Click `Deploy`.
   * Confirm the transaction in MetaMask.
   * Once deployed, copy the contract address from the `Deployed Contracts` section. Let's call this `ETH_MYTOKEN_ADDRESS`.
4. **Deploy `OATSBridge.sol`:**
   * Select `OATSBridge - contracts/OATSBridge.sol` from the `CONTRACT` dropdown.
   * In the `Deploy` field, enter the constructor arguments separated by a comma:
     * `gatewayAddress`: Enter [gateway address ](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)for Ethereum Sepolia here.&#x20;
     * `tokenAddress`: The `ETH_MYTOKEN_ADDRESS` you just copied.
   * Click `Deploy` and confirm in MetaMask.
   * Copy the deployed bridge address. Let's call this `ETH_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.

***

## Step 4: Deploy on Arbitrum Sepolia&#x20;

1. **Switch Network:** Change your MetaMask network to **Arbitrum Sepolia**.
2. **Deploy `MyToken.sol`:**
   * Follow the same steps as before to deploy `MyToken.sol`.
   * Copy the new token address. Let's call this `ARB_MYTOKEN_ADDRESS`.
3. **Deploy `OATSBridge.sol`:**
   * Select the `OATSBridge` contract.
   * Enter the constructor arguments:
     * `gatewayAddress`: Enter the [gateway address](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids) for Arbitrum Sepolia.
     * `tokenAddress`: The `ARB_MYTOKEN_ADDRESS` you just deployed.
   * Click `Deploy` and confirm in MetaMask.
   * Copy the deployed bridge address. Let's call this `ARB_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.

***

## Step 5: Configure and Fund the Bridges

The two bridges now need to be made aware of each other.

1. **Configure Ethereum Bridge:**
   * Switch MetaMask back to **Ethereum Sepolia**.
   * In Remix, under `Deployed Contracts`, find your `OATSBridge` at `ETH_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.
   * Expand its functions and find `setNetwork`.
   * Enter the following arguments:
     * `networkId`:  (Arbitrum Sepolia's ID). See[ **Gateway Addresses & Network IDs**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids) for more details.
     * `bridgeAddress`: The `ARB_BRIDGE_ADDRESS` you copied.
   * Click `transact` and confirm in MetaMask.
2. **Configure Arbitrum Bridge:**
   * Switch MetaMask to **Arbitrum Sepolia**.
   * Find your `OATSBridge` at `ARB_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.
   * In the `setNetwork` function, enter:
     * `networkId`:  (Ethereum Sepolia's ID). See[ **Gateway Addresses & Network IDs**](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids) for more details.
     * `bridgeAddress`: The `ETH_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.
   * Click `transact` and confirm.
3. **Fund the Arbitrum Bridge (Crucial Step):**
   * For the "**`unlock`**" to work, the bridge on Arbitrum must hold tokens. We will simulate this by sending it some of the `MyToken` we deployed on Arbitrum.
   * Find your `MyToken` contract at `ARB_MYTOKEN_ADDRESS`.
   * Expand its functions and use the `transfer` function to send tokens (e.g., `100000` tokens, remembering decimals: `100000000000000000000000`) to the `ARB_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.

***

## Step 6: Execute the Cross-Chain Transfer

Now, let's transfer 500 MTT from Ethereum Sepolia to Arbitrum Sepolia.

1. **Switch to Ethereum Sepolia:** Make sure your MetaMask is on the Ethereum Sepolia network.
2. **Approve Tokens:**
   * In Remix, find your `MyToken` contract at `ETH_MYTOKEN_ADDRESS`.
   * Expand the `approve` function and enter:
     * `spender`: The `ETH_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.
     * `amount`: The amount in wei (`500000000000000000000` for 500 tokens).
   * Click `transact` and confirm.
3. **Estimate Gas Cost:**
   * Find your `OATSBridge` contract at `ETH_BRIDGE_ADDRESS`.
   * Call the `cost` function with the `networkId` for Arbitrum Sepolia .
   * Copy the returned value (e.g., `2500000000000000`). This is the fee in wei.
4. **Send the Tokens:**
   * In the top section of Remix, set the `VALUE` field to the cost you just copied (in wei).
   * Expand the `send` function on the `OATSBridge` contract.
   * Enter the arguments:
     * `networkId`: [Arbitrum Sepolia ID.](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/gateway-addresses-and-network-ids)
     * `recipient`: Your own wallet address.
     * `amount`: The amount to send (`500000000000000000000`).
   * Click `transact`. MetaMask will pop up, asking you to confirm the transaction and pay both the standard gas fee and the `value` for the cross-chain fee. Confirm it.

***

## Step 7: Verify the Transfer

* **On Ethereum Sepolia:** Check your `MyToken` balance using the `balanceOf` function on the token contract. It should be reduced by 500. Check the bridge's balance; it should be increased by 500.
* **Wait:** The Analog network takes a few minutes to achieve consensus and execute the message on the destination chain.
* **On Arbitrum Sepolia:** Switch your MetaMask to Arbitrum Sepolia. Check your `MyToken` balance there. After a few minutes, you should see it has increased by 500. You can also check the transaction history of your wallet on the [Arbitrum Sepolia block explorer](https://sepolia.arbiscan.io/).

***

You have now successfully used an OATS-compliant bridge to transfer tokens from Ethereum Sepolia to Arbitrum Sepolia!
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}
