# Security Best Practices

To safeguard your node and staked assets, adhere to these critical practices:

**1. Server Hardening**

* **Minimal OS**: Use a lightweight, security-focused OS (e.g., Ubuntu Server LTS).
* **Updates**: Apply security patches and software updates immediately.
* **Unused Services**: Disable unnecessary ports, daemons, and user accounts.

**2. Network Security**

* **Firewalls**: Restrict inbound/outbound traffic to essential ports (e.g., P2P port `30333`).
* **SSH Security**:
  * Use SSH key authentication (disable password login).
  * Change the default SSH port and block brute-force attacks with tools like `fail2ban`.
* **VPN/Private Network**: Operate nodes in a private subnet or behind a VPN to limit exposure.

**3. Key Management**

* **Cold Wallets**: Store stash keys offline in hardware wallets (e.g., Ledger).
* **Staking Proxy/Stash Separation**: Use separate accounts:
  * **Stash account**: Holds funds; kept offline.
  * **Staking proxy**: Manages node operations; minimal balance.
* **Session Keys**: Rotate keys periodically and never store them on the server.

**4. Monitoring & Alerts**

* **Tools**: Deploy monitoring (e.g., Prometheus, Grafana) to track node health, RAM/CPU usage, and block production.
* **Alerts**: Set up notifications for downtime, disk space, or slashing risks (use tools like Cabal or Telegram bots).

**5. DDoS Mitigation**

* **Cloud Providers**: Host nodes only with DDoS-protected providers (e.g., AWS Shield, Google Cloud Armor).

**6. Backup & Recovery**

* **Regular Backups**: Automate backups of keystores, node data, and configurations.
* **Disaster Recovery Plan**: Test node restoration from backups to minimize downtime.

**7. Physical Security**

* If self-hosting, ensure servers are in a secure, access-controlled environment.

**8. Operational Vigilance**

* **Stay Informed**: Monitor Analog’s official channels for protocol upgrades or security advisories.
* **Phishing Scams**: Verify all links, software, and communications to avoid social engineering attacks.


# Troubleshooting

Here, we walk through common issues you might face when running a validator on Analog.

## 1. Docker Container Issues

### Symptom: Container fails to start

**Possible Causes:**

* Port conflicts (`30333/9944` already in use)
* Invalid volume mount path
* Missing `--validator` flag

**Solutions:**

1. Check for port conflicts:

   <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash"><strong>lsof -i :30333 &#x26;&#x26; lsof -i :9944
   </strong></code></pre>
2. Verify volume path exists and has write permissions:

   <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">chmod -R 755 /path/to/data
   </code></pre>
3. Ensure the `--validator` flag is included in the `docker run` command.

## 2. Chain Synchronization Issues

### Symptom: Node stuck at a block height

**Possible Causes:**

* Insufficient peers
* Corrupted chain data
* Warp Sync misconfiguration

**Solutions:**

1. Check peer count in logs:

   <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">docker logs [CONTAINER_ID] | grep "Discovered new peer"
   </code></pre>
2. Restart with **`Warp Sync:`**

   <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">docker run ... --sync=warp
   </code></pre>
3. Delete and resync chain data:

   <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">rm -rf /path/to/data/*
   </code></pre>

## 3. **RPC Connection Failures**

**Symptoms**:

* `author_rotateKeys` returns `Connection refused`
* Polkadot.js Apps fails to connect

**Solutions**:\
**A. Validate RPC Flags**:

* Confirm `--unsafe-rpc-external` and `--rpc-methods=unsafe` are included in the Docker command.

**B. Test RPC Endpoint**:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
curl -H "Content-Type: application/json" -d '{"id":1, "jsonrpc":"2.0", "method":"system_health"}' http://localhost:9944  
```

{% endcode %}

* If no response, check the firewall rules or Docker network settings.

## 4. **Validator Not Producing Blocks**

**Symptoms:**

* Node is bonded but shows `0 authored blocks` in Telemetry
* "No slot leader" in logs

**Solutions**:\
**A. Session Key Mismatch**:

* Regenerate and resubmit session keys:

{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```bash
echo '{"id":1,"jsonrpc":"2.0","method":"author_rotateKeys"}' | websocat -n1 ws://localhost:9944  
```

{% endcode %}

Update keys via Polkadot.js Apps **Network > Staking > Set Session Key**.

**B. Bonding/Payment Issues**:

* Ensure the bonded amount meets the [minimum requirement](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/?rpc=wss%3A%2F%2Frpc.timechain.analog.one#/staking/targets).
* Verify the reward destination account is valid (**Network > Staking > Accounts>Stashed**).

**C. Timechain Alignment**:

* Check system clock synchronization:

  <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">timedatectl status | grep "System clock synchronized"  
  </code></pre>
* Analog relies on precise timekeeping (6-second slots). Use NTP:

  <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">sudo apt install ntp; sudo systemctl restart ntp  
  </code></pre>

## **5. Validator Not in Active Set**

**Symptoms**:

* Node appears as "Waiting" in Telemetry
* No rewards after 12-hour nomination period

**Solutions**:\
**A. Stake Check**:

* Ensure the total bonded stake (self + nominations) ranks among the top *N* validators.

**B. Era Transition**:

* Validator sets update every 12 hours. Monitor era changes:

  <pre class="language-bash" data-overflow="wrap"><code class="lang-bash">docker logs [CONTAINER_ID] | grep "New era"  
  </code></pre>

**C. On-Chain Identity**:

* Register an on-chain identity (**Developer > Extrinsics > identity.setIdentity**) to increase visibility.

## 6. **High Resource Usage**

**Symptoms**:

* Node crashes with `Out of Memory`
* CPU usage >90% sustained

**Solutions**:\
**A. Hardware Requirements**:

* Check that your hardware meets [minimum requirements](/documentation/node-operators/introduction/technical-requirements#technical-requirements). For high nominations, scale vertically.

**B. Limit `Warp Sync` Impact**:

* **`Warp Sync`** uses heavy I/O. After syncing, restart without `--sync=warp`.

**C. Disable Non-Essential Services**:

* Remove `--prometheus-external` if not monitoring.

## 7. **Telemetry Not Reporting**

**Symptoms**:

* Node missing from [Analog Telemetry](wss://telemetry.analog.one)
* Logs show `Failed to submit telemetry`

**Solutions**:\
**A. Node Name Flag**:

* Confirm `--name 'YOUR_NODE_NAME'` is set and unique.

**B. Telemetry Endpoint**:

* Ensure no corporate firewall blocks outbound traffic to `wss://telemetry.analog.one`.


# Convert Fjord Presale to ANLOG

### Prerequisites

This guide assumes that you have the following components ready:

* EVM wallet address: This is the address you used on [Fjord Foundry](https://app.fjordfoundry.com/token-sales/0x34039FfB458BE16AE55015AC7bC730B2d253388a).
* Timechain address: For more details, read [Setting Up a Substrate Wallet](/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/wallets-and-accounts#setting-up-a-substrate-wallet)

{% hint style="info" %}
In addition to the wallet addresses, you must have claimed your $ANLOGPS (the placeholder presale tokens) on [Fjord Foundry](https://app.fjordfoundry.com/token-sales/0x34039FfB458BE16AE55015AC7bC730B2d253388a). These tokens reflect your presale allocation. They do not have any intrinsic monetary value and only indicate how many native $ANLOG tokens you’ll be eligible to receive on Timechain.
{% endhint %}

## Basic Steps

1. Go to the [official Burn/Swap Page](https://fjord.analog.one/). Connect your Ethereum wallet (containing $ANLOGPS) when prompted.
2. Enter your Timechain Wallet Address.
3. Follow the on-screen prompts to claim your native $ANLOG tokens.

{% hint style="success" %}
Claiming $ANLOG involves burning your Ethereum $ANLOGPS tokens (with wallet approval) and receiving the same amount of native $ANLOG on your Timechain Substrate address. Check your Substrate wallet for confirmation.
{% endhint %}

# Tooling

# Timechain Explorer

The [Timechain Explorer](https://explorer.analog.one/) provides a comprehensive view of activity on the Analog network, including:

* Verifying transactions and extrinsics.
* Monitoring blocks and account activity.
* Tracking cross-chain (i.e., GMP) messages. See [Track GMP Messages](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/track-gmp-messages) for more details.&#x20;

{% hint style="success" %}
The Timechain Explorer is available at <https://explorer.analog.one/> for Mainnet; and <https://explorer.testnet.analog.one/> for the Testnet.&#x20;
{% endhint %}

## Navigating the Explorer Tabs

The Explorer has various sections/tabs:&#x20;

<details>

<summary><strong>Home</strong></summary>

<img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2Fcx5sDU1kTQEBRQgi85Et%2FAnalog%20Explorer%20Home%20tab.png?alt=media&amp;token=0874e446-7c2a-4f29-9f6d-0e5403cc2e07" alt="" data-size="original">

Get a snapshot of the Timechain's health and overall stats, with details such as:&#x20;

* &#x20;**Chain data**, e.g., total blocks, total transfers, total extrinsics, and total accounts.
* &#x20;**Token metrics**, e.g., circulating supply and staked tokens.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Blocks</strong></summary>

View details about every block on the Timechain:

* **Block ID, Hash, Validator**: Identify blocks and their creators.
* **Status**: *Pending* (unconfirmed) or *Finalized* (confirmed).
* **Age, Extrinsics Count, Events**: Track when the block was added and its contents.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Extrinsics</strong></summary>

Extrinsics are on-chain actions (e.g., transactions, smart contract calls). Use this tab to debug transactions or verify execution. Each entry shows:

* **Extrinsic ID, Block Number, Hash**: Locate specific actions.
* **Result**: Success or failure.
* **Action**: Type of operation (e.g., timestamping).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Transfers</strong></summary>

Monitor token movements between accounts:

* **From/To Addresses, Value**: Track senders, receivers, and amounts.
* **Result**: Confirm if transfers succeeded.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>GMP</strong></summary>

This is your go-to tab for tracking cross-chain messages sent via the Analog GMP protocol. See [Track GMP Messages](/documentation/developers/analog-gmp/developers-guide/track-gmp-messages) for more details.&#x20;

* **Search**: You can search for GMP messages using:
  * **`Message ID`**: The unique identifier for the GMP payload. This is the primary identifier for tracking a message end-to-end.
  * **`Task ID`:** The Timechain's internal identifier for processing stages of the message.
  * **`Batch ID`**: If messages are processed in batches by the Timechain, this ID can find related messages.
* **Source Details:** Originating chain, contract address, or transaction hash on the source chain.
* **Destination Details:** Target chain, contract address, or transaction hash on the destination chain.
* **Message List Columns**:
  * **`Message ID`**: The unique identifier.
  * **`Source`**: Originating chain, contract, and source transaction hash.
  * **`Destination`**: Target chain, contract, and destination transaction hash (appears after execution).
  * **`Status`**: The current state of the message (e.g., Submitted, Processing, Completed, Failed).
  * **`Created At`**: Timestamp when the message was initiated on the source chain.
  * **`Updated At`**: Timestamp of the last status update.
* **GMP Telemetry Pane:**
  * **GMP Pane**: GMP messages. You can sort messages via **Read Message Tasks** or **Submit Gateway Tasks**.
  * **Chronicles Pane**: Details about individual Chronicle nodes (Account ID, connected networks, signature, state, assigned Shard ID, last heartbeat).
  * **Shards Pane**: Information about active shards, the networks they service, and their current state.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Staking</strong></summary>

Monitor the health and activity of the Timechain's consensus mechanism.

* **Details**: Number of active validators, nomination pool information, current era and epoch numbers, and total staking amounts (validator stake vs. nominator stake).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Accounts</strong></summary>

Look up details for specific accounts on the Timechain.

* **Details**: Account address, number of extrinsics initiated, and token balance.

</details>


# Polkadot.Js

&#x20;[Polkadot.Js ](https://wiki.polkadot.network/docs/polkadotjs)is a suite of tools that allows Substrate-based networks to interact granularly with the Polkadot blockchain. One of the main components of Polkadot.Js is the [Polkadot.Js Apps](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/), which includes libraries that facilitate interaction with Substrate-based networks via WebSocket endpoints.

## How to use Polkadot.Js Apps to Interact with Analog Network

Analog is a Substrate-based network, which means it can be used with any Substrate-based network block explorer. Polkadot.Js Apps can be used to explore and interact with either the Analog Testnet or Analog Main-net (also called Analog Timechain).&#x20;

{% hint style="warning" %}
Note that when you launch [Polkadot.Js Apps](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/) for the first time, you may or may not be connected to your desired network (i.e., Analog Testnet or Analog Timechain). For more details about these networks, check out [Network Information](/documentation/analog-network/network-information). See below for steps you'll need to follow to connect to Analog Testnet or Analog Timechain.
{% endhint %}

### Analog Testnet

Follow these steps to connect to Analog Testnet:

1. Launch your browser and point it to this URL: <https://polkadot.js.org/apps/>.
2. Navigate to the top left corner of the page and click on the logo. You should see a list of networks organized by main-nets, testnets, and local networks.
3. Locate the “***Testnets***” category and select “Analog Testnet.”

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FFwR1GnDQjaeydQpgJzsV%2FScreenshot%202024-05-06%20at%2010.03.34.png?alt=media&amp;token=45298d2b-9d50-4dcd-a55b-71e3c2c1248d" alt=""><figcaption><p>How to connect to Analog Testnet</p></figcaption></figure>

4. Once you’ve selected “***Analog Testnet***,” scroll back to the top and click the “***Switch***” button. From now on, you should be able to connect to Analog Testnet whenever you launch [Polkadot.Js Apps](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/).

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2F3vTPQRIpiHzjL7g4y2WE%2FScreenshot%202024-05-06%20at%2010.19.35.png?alt=media&amp;token=b643565a-1c2a-4bf1-ba1a-ea909c327c4b" alt=""><figcaption><p>Analog Testnet</p></figcaption></figure>

### Analog Timechain

Follow these steps to connect to Analog Timechain:

1. Launch your browser and point it to this URL: <https://polkadot.js.org/apps/>.
2. Navigate to the top left corner of the page and click on the logo. You should see a list of networks organized by mainnets, testnets, and local networks.
3. Locate the “***Live Networks***” category and select “***Analog Timechain.***”

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FrOdrUzfahFHbcBjrGpD2%2FPolkadot%20Js%20Aanalog%20Timechain.png?alt=media&amp;token=0dcbb9fa-3ebb-415b-8169-bbfeaa15a40c" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

4. Once you’ve selected “***Analog Timechain***,” scroll back to the top and click the “***Switch***” button. From now on, you should be able to connect to Analog Testnet whenever you launch [Polkadot.Js Apps](https://polkadot.js.org/apps/).

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2Fogfero3Q1JZhypYPweTR%2FTimechain%20Explorer.png?alt=media&amp;token=dcfad16c-5fbd-4ce9-ad65-22f88dff09e5" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>


# Utilities

Tools to simplify and expedite DApp development


# Session Key Generator

Session Key Generator helps you generate session keys for signing purposes when deploying and querying Views through the Watch SDK.

{% hint style="info" %}
NOTE

You can use the `keygen`, `@polkadot/extension-dapp/` or `@polkadot/Keyring` packages to generate new session keys. For detailed instructions, refer to the *"*[*Quickstart*](https://docs.analog.one/documentation/developers/analog-watch/quickstart)*"* guide
{% endhint %}

## Prerequisites <a href="#before-you-begin" id="before-you-begin"></a>

This guide assumes you've already set up the following requisites:

1. Need an account in any substrate-based wallets like [Polkadot.JS](https://polkadot.js.org/extension/), [SubWallet](https://www.subwallet.app/), [Enkrypt](https://www.enkrypt.com/#overview), and [Talisman](https://www.talisman.xyz/). Refer to [Wallet Support](https://docs.analog.one/documentation/developers/analog-watch/developers-guide/wallet-support) for more details
2. Need the latest version of [npm](https://nodejs.org/en) or [yarn](https://yarnpkg.com/getting-started/install)

## Installation <a href="#install-the-watch-client-and-wasm-package" id="install-the-watch-client-and-wasm-package"></a>

Install the Watch Client (timegraph-js) and the WASM Package (timegraph-wasm) using your preferred package manager

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="npm" %}
{% code lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
npm i @analog-labs/timegraph-js
npm i @analog-labs/timegraph-wasm
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="yarn" %}
{% code lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
yarn add @analog-labs/timegraph-js
yarn add @analog-labs/timegraph-wasm
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

The Watch Client offers client-side features for Analog Watch interaction, while the WASM Package contains efficient WebAssembly compiled code that runs directly in the browser or Node.js environment.

## Authentication

The WASM Package supports both the web and node environments. The authentication process involves five steps: &#x20;

### 1. Create a certificate

Create a `certificate` using your Substrate-supported wallet address as follows:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import init, { new_cert } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm';

// initialize the Web Assembly in the JS Environment.
init();

// <Wallet Address> must be polkadot/substrate supported.
// <Role> can be "user" or "developer"
const [certificate, secret] = new_cert('<Wallet Address>', '<Role>'); 
```

{% endcode %}

Notice that `init()` can only be used in a web environment.&#x20;
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Node" %}

<pre class="language-javascript" data-overflow="wrap" data-line-numbers><code class="lang-javascript">const { new_cert } =  require('@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm');
<strong>// &#x3C;Wallet Address> must be polkadot/substrate supported.
</strong><strong>// &#x3C;Role> can be user or developer
</strong><strong>const [certificate, secret] = new_cert('&#x3C;Wallet Address>', '&#x3C;Role>');
</strong></code></pre>

{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

<details>

<summary><strong>Response</strong></summary>

```json
[
    [0,0,0,1,0,0,0,32,0,0,0.......]
    "9yPmWeRLcpGn9rAeqg8npRyBmgA9D8D94KPTpfkD8Kgd"
]
```

</details>

### 2. Setup the wallet&#x20;

Install the necessary packages to sign the wallet

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="npm" %}

```
npm i @polkadot/extension-dapp @polkadot/util @polkadot/util-crypto
```

{% endtab %}

{% tab title="yarn" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" %}

```javascript
yarn add @polkadot/extension-dapp @polkadot/util  @polkadot/util-crypto
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

Paste the below code in your IDE,

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}

<pre class="language-javascript" data-overflow="wrap" data-line-numbers><code class="lang-javascript"><strong>const { Keyring } = require('@polkadot/keyring');
</strong>const { addFromJson } = require('@polkadot/util');

const keyring = new Keyring({ type: 'sr25519' });
const keypair = keyring.addFromJson(/* JSON object obtained from exporting the wallet goes here */);
keypair.unlock("&#x3C;PASSWORD>");
</code></pre>

{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Node" %}

<pre class="language-javascript" data-overflow="wrap" data-line-numbers><code class="lang-javascript"><strong>const { Keyring } = require('@polkadot/keyring');
</strong>const { addFromJson } = require('@polkadot/util');

const keyring = new Keyring({ type: 'sr25519' });
const keypair = keyring.addFromJson(/* JSON object obtained from exporting the wallet goes here */);
keypair.unlock("&#x3C;PASSWORD>");
</code></pre>

{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

### 3. Generate the API key&#x20;

To proceed, follow these steps

1. Get `keypair` from above "setup the wallet" step
2. Get `secret` & `certificate` from first step "create a certificate" step

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import { build_apikey } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm';
import { injector } from '@polkadot/extension-dapp'
import { hexToU8a } from '@polkadot/util';

const signedData = await injector.signer.signRaw({
    address: "<wallet address>",
    data: certificate, // get from "create a certificate" step
    type: "bytes",
});

const apiKey = build_apikey(secret,certificate, hexToU8a(signedData.signature));
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Node" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
const { build_apikey } = require('@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm'); 
const { cryptoWaitReady } = require('@polkadot/util-crypto');

async function run(){
    await cryptoWaitReady();
    
    const signedData = keypair.sign(certificate); // sign the wallet
    const apiKey = build_apikey(secret, certificate, signedData); // build the apiKey

    // continue below steps from here...
    
}
run();
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

<details>

<summary><strong>Response</strong></summary>

```json
"apiKey":{
   "key":"HtACKxQe9dx4Xmg8d1sSd7PQnzvNKSS9ViBb2Aq8Fdo",
   "secret":"9yPmWeRLcpGn9rAeqg8npRyBmgA9D8D94KPTpfkD8Kgd",
   "cert":"111CfKkxwN6abhKrnZ4wKEWdpagvfNqe6iUR5Gg....."
}
```

</details>

### 4. Generate the session key (SSK)

Follow these steps to generate the session key (SSK):

1. Encode SSK with wallet address and UserId.
2. Sign the encoded SSK using the Substrate wallet signer.
3. Build the SSK.

The code below illustrates these processes:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import init, { encode_ssk, build_ssk  } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm'
import {injector } from '@polkadot/extension-dapp'
import { hexToU8a} from '@polkadot/util';
import { encode_ssk, build_ssk } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm';

const sskData = encode_ssk({
      ns: 0,
      key: '<Wallet Address>',
      user_id: 1,
      expiration: 0,
});

const sskSignature = await injector.signer.signRaw({
      address: '<Wallet Address>',
      data: ssk_data,
      type: 'bytes',
});

const ssk = build_ssk(sskData, hexToU8a(sskSignature.signature));
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Node" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
const { encode_ssk, build_ssk } = require('@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm');

const sskData = encode_ssk({
    ns: 0,
    key: keypair.address, // <Wallet Address>
    user_id: 1,
    expiration: 0,
});

const signedSSKData = keypair.sign(sskData); // sign ssk data
const ssk = build_ssk(sskData, signedSSKData); // build ssk
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

<details>

<summary><strong>Response</strong></summary>

```json
{
   "ssk":"0;EFVS6JNJ7ah615F69cTHsDrUP2vziy1943hyW6LUbkfr;0;*;1;26528g8cdQfzBw2ue85Hbkcw3cpRmrfrhjhpEScxeKhQuVMYCKzoucNcXUmXEiypg9G7188n9h2pzLCRwD1BFFk3"
}
```

</details>

### 5. Create an account

With the SSK data generated, you can now create an account by providing the wallet address and SSK to the SDK:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import { TimegraphClient } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-js';

const timegraphClient = new TimegraphClient({
    url: '<URL>',
    sessionKey: '<SSK>',
});

// paste below code snippet into the async function
const createUser = await timegraphClient.user.create({ address: '<wallet address>' });
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Node" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
const { TimegraphClient } = require('@analog-labs/timegraph-js');

const timegraphClient = new TimegraphClient({
    url: '<URL>',
    sessionKey: '<SSK>',
});

// paste below code snippet into the async function
const createUser = await timegraphClient.user.create({ address: '<wallet address>' });
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

<details>

<summary><strong>Response</strong></summary>

```json
"response":{
   {
      "userId":111,
      "wallet":"5GWqFcdT8ugZW8fZhZHQzcx96M2jQYLV7M9KMozYPzLfEdR9",
      "userRole":"developer",
      "subgraph":111
   }
}
```

</details>

### 6. Validate the API key certificate

To validate the certificate data for a new API key and save it in storage, you must pass a valid SSK (Session Signing Key) for the signing account as follows:

1. Ensure you have an existing account in the Watch Portal.
2. The SSK can be downloaded by clicking on the download icon next to the wallet address in the "[**Profile**](https://watch.testnet.analog.one/#/profile)" section of the Watch Portal.&#x20;

The code below illustrates these processes:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import { TimegraphClient } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-js';

const timegraphClient = new TimegraphClient({
    url: '<URL>', // timegraph GraphQL endpoint (ex: timrgraph.testnet.analog.one/graphql)
    sessionKey: '<SSK>', // valid <SSK> 
});

// paste below code snippet into the async function
const certifyKey = await timegraphClient.apiKey.certify({ cert: '<certificate>' }); // Take <certificate> from above generated apiKey
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}

{% tab title="Node" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
const { TimegraphClient } = require('@analog-labs/timegraph-js');

const timegraphClient = new TimegraphClient({
    url: '<URL>', // timegraph GraphQL endpoint (ex: timrgraph.testnet.analog.one/graphql)
    sessionKey: '<SSK>', // valid <SSK> 
});

// paste below code snippet into the async function
const certifyKey = await timegraphClient.apiKey.certify({ cert: '<certificate>' }); // Take <certificate> from above generated apiKey
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}
{% endtabs %}

<details>

<summary><strong>Response</strong></summary>

```json
"response":{
   "certifyKey":{
      "userId":100:"111CfKkxwN6abhKrnZ4wKEWdpagvfNqe6iUR5Gg.....",
      "key":"HtACKxQe9dx4Xmg8d1sSd7PQnzvNKSS9ViBb2Aq8Fdo",
      "role":"developer",
      "status":"active"
   }
}
```

</details>

### Entire Session Key Generation in one go

The code below illustrates the whole ssk generation process:

You have to change two things manually in below code snippet

1. Connect wallet
2. Existed user session key&#x20;

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import { Keyring } from "@polkadot/keyring";
import { TimegraphClient } from "@analog-labs/timegraph-js";
import {cryptoWaitReady} from "@polkadot/util-crypto";
import { new_cert, encode_ssk, build_ssk, build_apikey } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-wasm';

const timegraphGraphqlUrl = "https://timegraph.testnet.analog.one/graphql";

async function sessionKeyGeneration(){
  await cryptoWaitReady();
  const keyring = new Keyring({ type: "sr25519" });

  const keypair = keyring.addFromJson(/* JSON object obtained from exporting the wallet goes here */);
  keypair.unlock("<PASSWORD>");

  // build api key
  const [certificate, secret] = new_cert(keypair.address, "developer"); 
  const signedData = keypair.sign(certificate);
  const apiKey = build_apikey(secret,certificate, signedData);
  
  // if user is not existed for above wallet then create new using existed timegraph user otherwise comment out below two steps
  const validTimegraphUser = new TimegraphClient({
    url: timegraphGraphqlUrl,
    sessionKey: "<Existed_User_Valid_SSK>",
  });

  // create a new user
  await validTimegraphUser.user.create({ address: keypair.address })

  const sskData = encode_ssk({
    ns: 0,
    key: keypair.address,
    user_id: 1,
    expiration: 0 // expiration time in milliseconds
  });

  const signedSSKData = keypair.sign(sskData);
  const ssk = build_ssk(sskData, signedSSKData);
  
  const newTimegraphUser = new TimegraphClient({
    url: timegraphGraphqlUrl,
    sessionKey: ssk,
  });

  // store api key in timegraph's new user account
  await newTimegraphUser.apiKey.certify({ cert: apiKey.cert });

  // print ssk
  console.log(ssk)

  // check the ssk
  console.log(await newTimegraphUser.apiKey.list())
  
}

sessionKeyGeneration();
```

{% endcode %}
{% endtab %}

### Enable the previously revoked API key

You can also enable the previously revoked API key by passing a valid session key for the key's owner like this:

{% tabs %}
{% tab title="Web" %}
{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import { TimegraphClient } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-js';
import { cryptoWaitReady } from '@polkadot/util-crypto';

const timegraphClient = new TimegraphClient({
    url: '<URL>',
    sessionKey: '<SSK>',
});

// paste below code snippet into the async function
const enableAPIKey = timegraphClient.apiKey.enable({ key: '<Api key>' });
```

### Revoke an API Key

Likewise, you can also revoke an API key by passing a valid SSK for the key's owner in the Authorization header:

{% tabs %}

{% code overflow="wrap" lineNumbers="true" %}

```javascript
import { TimegraphClient } from '@analog-labs/timegraph-js';

const timegraphClient = new TimegraphClient({
    url: '<URL>',
    sessionKey: '<SSK>',
});

// paste below code snippet into the async function
const revokeAPIKey = await timegraphClient.apiKey.revoke({ key: '<Api key>' });
```

# Tokenomics

As a sovereign Nominated Proof-of-Stake (NPoS) blockchain, Analog utilizes its native $ANLOG token (ticker: $ANLOG) for core functions: rewarding stakers, enabling ecosystem transactions, and covering network fees. See the [Timepaper](https://www.analog.one/Analog-Timepaper.pdf) for more details on network mechanics and the [Tokenomics Paper ](https://docs.google.com/document/d/e/2PACX-1vRCpMd7VmDxqGYXYZfj-1RyHdWRU6DfBf1tTad4vlpmmvp4Coavwh9mmT42Te665A/pub)for an overview of the $ANLOG token’s economics.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FJZ2Ni8l5TzIRWbjCyQJT%2FCirculating%20Supply.png?alt=media&amp;token=75cc26d1-b716-4d1a-8638-ea47a4877ff0" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

## Token Utility

The $ANLOG token powers the Analog ecosystem, enabling interoperability, security, and governance. Its core and extended utilities include:

### Core Utilities

1. **Staking & Network Security:**&#x20;

* Node Operators (i.e., validators) stake $ANLOG to participate in the NPoS consensus, securing the Timechain.
* Nominators delegate $ANLOG to validators, earning a share of staking rewards.
* Slashing mechanisms penalize malicious actors, ensuring protocol integrity.

2. **Transaction Fees:**&#x20;

* All Timechain operations (transfers, staking, governance voting, etc.) require fees paid in $ANLOG.

3. **Protocol Fees:**

* Used as collateral for dApps (e.g., [Analog Watch](/documentation/developers/analog-watch/developers-guide/rewards-and-fees)).
* Cross-chain protocols (e.g., Analog GMP/Automation) may support native gas tokens for user flexibility while prioritizing $ANLOG for ecosystem alignment.

4. **Governance:**

* Holders vote on protocol upgrades, treasury allocations, and ecosystem development.

### Liquidity Provider Incentives

LPs holding $ANLOG tokens can access attractive incentives across various Analog products and services, balancing competitive returns with sustainable token economics. For example, LPs that provide liquidity for [Zenswap](https://zenswap.io/) earn fees and incentives from cross-chain swaps and optimizing low-slippage asset transfers.

## $WANLOG

**The $ANLOG token is interoperable, operating across multiple blockchains.** On EVM chains (such as Ethereum), it utilizes the ERC-20 standard for **$WANLOG**. At TGE, a percentage of the $ANLOG's total token supply was minted on the Ethereum blockchain as $WANLOG.

As a holder of $WANLOG tokens, you can perform all standard actions associated with an ERC20 token, with the added capability of transferring your tokens across different EVM-based chains.

**Key Features:**

* **Standard ERC20:** You can `transfer` tokens to other users and `approve` other smart contracts to spend tokens on your behalf.
* **Cross-Chain Transfers:** You can seamlessly move your $WANLOG tokens from one supported blockchain to another.

**Token Basics:**

* **Name**: Wrapped Analog One Token
* **Symbol**: **`$WANLOG`**
* **Decimals**: `12`
* **Capped Supply**: Set during initialization.

## Token Metrics

| Property                  | Details                                                                                                                                                                                           |
| ------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Ticker                    | $ANLOG                                                                                                                                                                                            |
| Total supply (at genesis) | 9,057,971,000                                                                                                                                                                                     |
| Inflation model           | Future rewards will be funded by inflation, projected at 1% to 4% annually. However, current validator rewards are funded by a 2% pre-allocation from the Future Initiatives Pool, not inflation. |
| Decimals                  | 12                                                                                                                                                                                                |
| Base unit                 | $$TOCK$$                                                                                                                                                                                          |
| Conversion                | $$1 TOCK = ANLOG \* 10^{-12}$$                                                                                                                                                                    |

## Distribution Framework (Genesis Block)

$ANLOG's total supply is fixed at **9,057,971,000**. A percentage of this supply was minted at the mainnet launch (see current circulating supply [here](https://explorer.analog.one/)), and the rest will be vested and distributed as follows.

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2Fjh4ajT6J0cKDTvfuPhyx%2FToken%20Allocation%20Chart%20.png?alt=media&amp;token=049dd5d6-fd84-48c4-b150-7c7ae0ac918c" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

## Vesting Schedule

The vesting schedule is engineered to encourage long-term stability within the Analog ecosystem and commitment to the community. The graph below outlines the gradual release of ANLOG tokens to each stakeholder over time:

<figure><img src="https://3751219372-files.gitbook.io/~/files/v0/b/gitbook-x-prod.appspot.com/o/spaces%2FMINNcjmm2i5Xcankmyyd%2Fuploads%2FydBjGKakLwZ2POyzeNyl%2FAnalog%20Release%20Schedule.png?alt=media&amp;token=44adc925-1af0-48e7-9d7e-4b3d3f41394f" alt=""><figcaption></figcaption></figure>

{% hint style="success" %}
A key feature of this schedule is the implementation of a 9-month lock-up and 36-month vesting period for Team/Advisors. However, Community pool tokens (e.g., from Airdrop and Ecosystem & Growth) are unlocked immediately after the main-net launch. A percentage of these tokens (i.e., from the Community pool) will be immediately available for Analog Network launch and operations, with the remainder vesting over 48 months.&#x20;

Importantly, ***all tokens, regardless of lock-up status, can be used for staking***.
{% endhint %}

# Claiming ANLOG Tokens

This guide explains how to claim your vested $ANLOG tokens, which are essential for staking, governance, and accessing ecosystem utilities. Tokens are released based on your vesting schedule (see [Tokenomics](/documentation/resources/tokenomics#vesting-schedule)). Ensure you claim them promptly to participate in network security, governance, and liquidity programs.

## Prerequisites

**Before claiming:**

1. **Email Link**: Access the Claim Portal via the **unique link** sent to your registered email.
2. A **Timechain-compatible wallet** (e.g., Polkadot.js, Talisman, or SubWallet with Analog Network configuration).
3. Your **whitelisted wallet address** used during the token sale.
4. If you haven’t submitted your wallet address, [complete this form](https://l5d87lam6fy.typeform.com/to/hO4VX6IK).

## Step-by-Step Claim Process

1. **Access the Claim Portal:**
   1. Click the **unique link** in your email to access the portal.
   2. Always verify the URL to avoid phishing.
2. **Connect Your Wallet**
   1. Click “**Connect Wallet**” on the Claim Portal and select your wallet provider (e.g., Polkadot.js, SubWallet, Talisman, etc.).
   2. Ensure the wallet is connected to the Analog Timechain.
3. **Wallet Connection Flow**

**Scenario A: Wallet Already Whitelisted:**

1. If your wallet is **whitelisted**, the portal will detect your wallet and display:
   1. Total allocated tokens.
   2. Vested tokens available to claim (based on your vesting schedule).
   3. Vesting progress bar and next unlock date.
2. Review your details and click “Verify” to confirm your wallet and complete whitelisting.

**Scenario B: Wallet Not Whitelisted:**

If your wallet is **not whitelisted**, you’ll see an error when you attempt to connect the wallet.

1. Fill out this [form](https://l5d87lam6fy.typeform.com/to/hO4VX6IK) to:
   1. Provide your name, wallet address, and other investment details.
2. Submit the form. The Analog team will review your submission within 7 days.

**Scenario C: Incorrect Vesting Details:**

If your token allocation or vesting schedule is inaccurate:&#x20;

1. Click “Contact Us” on the Claim Portal.

2. You’ll be redirected to this [form](https://l5d87lam6fy.typeform.com/to/wEOHF84q), where you’ll:&#x20;
   1. Provide correct details (e.g., wallet address, investment amount, etc.).

3. Submit the form. The team will update your details within 7 days.

4. **Claim Tokens**
   1. If tokens are available, click “Claim”.
   2. Confirm the transaction in your wallet.
   3. Tokens will reflect in your wallet after network confirmation.

## FAQ

### 1. How do I check my vesting schedule?

After connecting your wallet, the Token Dashboard displays your allocation details, including cliff periods, vesting progress, and next unlock dates.

### 2. What if I never received a claim email?

Verify your spam folder. If unresolved, submit this [form](https://l5d87lam6fy.typeform.com/to/hO4VX6IK).&#x20;

### 3. What if I used the wrong wallet address?

Submit the correct address and proof of participation via this [form](https://l5d87lam6fy.typeform.com/to/wEOHF84q). The Analog team will update your records within 7 business days.

### 4. Why are my tokens not available yet?

* Your tokens may still be in the cliff period (e.g., Seed Round: 9 months).
* Post-cliff, tokens unlock linearly (e.g., 1/27th monthly for Seed Round).

### 5. Can I claim tokens before the cliff ends?

No. Tokens are locked during the cliff period and only become claimable afterward.

### 6. Which wallets are supported?

Timechain-native wallets (e.g., Polkadot.js, SubWallet, Talisman, etc.). See [Supported Wallets](https://docs.analog.one/documentation/analog-network/getting-started/supported-wallets) for more details.&#x20;

### 7. What happens if I don’t claim tokens?

Unclaimed tokens remain in your vesting pallet but won’t auto-transfer. Claim them periodically to use or stake.

<br>

# Timepaper

Today’s blockchain ecosystems function like walled gardens. Liquidity—the lifeblood of decentralized finance (DeFi)—is trapped within individual chains, leading to inefficient capital utilization, higher transaction costs, and slippage. Users face friction navigating multiple wallets and bridges, while developers are confined to building within single-chain limitations. Cross-chain interactions rely on risky, centralized bridges, exposing users to security vulnerabilities and complexity.

Analog is an interoperability layer that acts as a connective fabric, enabling autonomous blockchains to communicate seamlessly while retaining sovereignty. By unifying liquidity and enabling secure asset/data transfer across chains, this layer transforms fragmented ecosystems into a cohesive network. The [Analog Timepaper ](https://www.analog.one/Analog-Timepaper.pdf)describes the mechanisms powering the protocol.


# Glossary

Here is a list of common terms used on the Analog Network:

## Analog Watch

<details>

<summary><strong>View</strong></summary>

A View is a set of data aggregated from single or multiple sources (i.e., smart contacts) across one or numerous blockchains and stored as a table in a relational database.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>View Definition</strong></summary>

View Definition describes the structure of the resulting View and the relationship between the various entities. It specifies how data from the supported chains should be indexed, making it queryable by applications.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Cycle</strong></summary>

A cycle refers to the frequency with which Analog Watch refreshes the data after the View has been sponsored.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Data Source</strong></summary>

A smart contract on an external chain from which the View sources data.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>TSS Data Hash</strong></summary>

A cryptographic output derived from combined signatures of a designated shard and hashed transactions from external chains submitted to the Timechain for further validation and storage.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Subgraph</strong></summary>

A high-level descriptor detailing the exact smart contract functions and Views associated with a session key.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Timechain</strong></summary>

An immutable ledger maintained by the Analog Network validators (i.e., Time Nodes). It cryptographically stores the metadata and proofs associated with smart contracts and Views, guaranteeing that the available Analog Watch data is valid and tamper-resistant.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Data Designers</strong></summary>

Entities that list smart contracts and Views on the Analog Watch.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Data Collectors</strong></summary>

Entities that fund/sponsor the indexing and processing of Views.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Data Consumers</strong></summary>

Users/applications of Analog Watch that explore and query published Views.

</details>

## Analog GMP

<details>

<summary><strong>General Message Passing (GMP)</strong></summary>

The ability of a DApp or smart contract on one chain, like Astar, to invoke an action on another chain, such as Polygon.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Cross-Chain</strong></summary>

The ability of a DApp or smart contract to transfer data/messages and tokens (value) between different blockchains.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Multi-chain</strong></summary>

A growing Web3 ecosystem that is characterized by multiple blockchain networks, and it includes DApps that are launched on numerous chains.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Cross-Chain Composability</strong></summary>

The ability for A DApp/smart contract on one chain to seamlessly communicate with other DApps/smart contracts on different chains, allowing developers to freely use, reassemble, modify, and build new applications or services.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Timechain</strong></summary>

An NPoS protocol maintained by dynamic and decentralized Time Nodes.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Time Nodes</strong></summary>

On-chain validators that secure the Timechain by verifying and committing new blocks.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Chronicle Nodes</strong></summary>

Off-chain validators that provide attestation services (i.e., monitoring inbound cross-chain requests from connected chains, attesting to their validity, and posting the signed payload to the Timechain), and relaying services (i.e., picking up the request from the Timechain and forwarding them to the destination chain for execution).

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Shard</strong></summary>

An independent network within the Chronicle Node Network, which vertically divides the entire set of Chronicle Nodes. It enables multi-chain operability within the Analog Network ecosystem.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Threshold signature scheme (TSS)</strong></summary>

A digital signature mechanism that generates a single signature from multiple Chronicle Nodes within a shard, with the signature distributed to ensure no single Chronicle Node operator has complete control over the private key.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Multi-Party Computation (MPC)</strong></summary>

A cryptographic primitive used in the TSS that enables multiple Chronicle Nodes within a shard to sign off on cross-chain requests without disclosing their individual inputs.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Relayer</strong></summary>

A Chronicle Node that gets selected on the destination shard to execute GMP message on that chain, following instructions emanating from the Timechain.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Gateways</strong></summary>

Also called **Gateway Smart Contracts (GSCs)**: They are contracts that Analog Network manages on each supported blockchain. They expose interfaces that developers can use to send and receive GMP messages.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Gateway Smart Contracts (GSCs)</strong></summary>

See ***Gateways***.

</details>

<details>

<summary><strong>Oracle Service</strong></summary>

A service that each deployed GSC runs and is continuously updated to provide data about gas prices for different tokens on the supported chains.

</details>
